01

Introduction & Meaning of Comparative Government and Politics

Video Part 1 · starts at 00:04

Introducing Comparative Government & Politics

  • Now moving to the course of comparative governing politics.
  • As I said it is a very important domain in the subject matter of political science. Political science has various subsets. It could be international relations.

Political Theory, Peace Studies & Comparative Politics

  • It is political theory.
  • It is peace and conflict studies and one of them is comparative government and politics.
  • When we look at comparative government and politics, we see it from a very sharp we look at we look at it from a very sharp analysis that is government and politics and that to comparison.
  • So comparative government and politics compares different countries governments and its political environment or its political system.
  • Today the lesson we’re going to discuss is the first subtopic that is the meaning of comparative government and politics in module one and this is a very important course in semester 3.
  • At the end of this session you will be able to define the meaning of comparative government and politics.
  • You’ll also be able to describe why these are important constituent of politics.
  • Now coming to the meaning of concept of comparative government in politics.

The Meaning of ‘Comparative’

  • As I said when we speak about the term comparative in literal terms it will always refer to as a comparison.
  • Whenever you involve the term comparison, it will always be two countries, two states, particularly in the subject domain of comparative government and politics.
  • Comparative government and politics are two different subjects.
  • Yet the modern scientist have laid emphasis on the combined study of both because they both are very closely related.
  • In certain philosophers views you will be looking at comparative government and politics from the same perspective.
  • But in some others viewpoint you’ll be looking at from a very different perspective altogether.
  • Apart from this comparative method has been used for the study of both the subjects.

The Comparative Method & Its Parameters

  • So both government and politics use only one method and that is the comparative method.
  • Because when you compare two countries with each other, you need certain parameters on which one will be working.
  • Aristotle who is also considered to be the father of political science has made use of comparative method to understand and comprehend the political problem of his time.
  • So what could be the lack in certain political systems?
  • Aristotle, Plato have all talked about comparative governments, have all talked about why certain country is considered to be developing and why the other is supposed to be developed.
  • Aristotle’s book politics was a result of the comparative study of the constitutional system of 158 city states. So we can see how descriptive his study was.
  • 58 states and they were compared in terms of parameters like their constitution, their political system, their political bureaucracy etc.

Comparative Government vs Comparative Politics

  • Now what is the concept of comparative government?
  • So we’ll be looking at comparative politics differently and comparative government differently.
  • Comparative government includes a theoretical study of the subjects like forms of government, organs of government, their organization and their functions.
  • But no attention is to be paid towards the study of formal and informal factors which influence the working of the government.
  • Neither the study of political behavior nor of political processes etc. has made the subject matter of comparative government.
  • So whenever we study government, we only study its forms, its structure, its organs.
  • Rest who are the members, how they have joined is nothing related to comparative.

Internal vs External Parameters of Comparison

  • So two countries even if they’re compared external parameters will never be judged. It is only the internal parameters. That is why forms, organs and functions.
  • Now coming to the concept of comparative politics.
  • Comparative politics is not only concerned with the formal institutions rather it is also concerned with non-formal institutions.
  • So while comparative government focused only on formal institutions the organ the function and the structure but comparative politics will look to those parameters which comparative government have not covered.
  • So that means it is really an extension of comparative government.

Elections, Electoral Behaviour & Non-Governmental Factors

  • Comparative politics will be witnessing elections, electoral behavior, processions, non-governmental factors.
  • Non-governmental factors could be civil society organizations, NOS’s, tribal groups, language, religious organization and its impact on politics.
  • The definitions pertaining to comparative government and politics.
  • Generally it is seen that various scholars use the concept of comparative politics and comparative government, comparative constitutions, comparative political systems interchangeably.
  • But these concepts are vastly different from each other.
  • According to Edward Freeman, comparative politics is a comparative analysis of the various forms of government and diverse political institutions.
  • As I also highlighted earlier, non-political institutions are also involved.
  • According to Michael Curtis, comparative politics is concerned with significant regularities, similarities, differences in the working of political institutions and in political behavior.
  • Coming to the characteristics of comparative government and politics.
  • There are several analytical and empirical investigation.

Formal & Informal Structures

  • Comparative study of the formal and informal structures. Study of infrastructures. Emphasis on the study of developing societies.
  • Emphasis on the interdisciplinary method of study.
  • value-free study. quest of theory building and horizontal and vertical comparison.
  • So all these are characteristics how one government or one political system is compared with the other.
  • These are the factors through which they are coordinated through which they are distinguished.
  • Comparative politics has been very important in the field of political science.
  • The study of governments is a significant part of the study of politics.
  • The comparative study of government and politics is an essential requirement for the field of political science.

Nature of Comparative Politics

  • The nature of comparative politics seeks to analyze and compare different political systems that work under different societies.
  • One of the most important challenges in political science was to develop a broadly applicable theory of the political system.
  • Because earlier political science already had subsets like political theory which is developed concepts which is talking about international relations but there was none such theory in political science which literally talked about political systems. This theory was developed by David Easton.
  • The outputs of a political system are authoritative decisions and actions of the political authorities for the distribution and division of values.
  • Now what is the significance of study of governments in politics?
  • The pragmatic evolution of the governmental politics or political system of own country is made possible by recognizing the governmental processes or the country’s political system.

Objective & Rational Study of Governments

  • A comparative study of governments not only streamlines the progress of objective and rational judgment but at the same time in terms of political system disperses the dangerously ambiguous forms of ethnocentrism that one’s own country is superior to the other.
  • The structure and behavior of government makes an exciting and challenging area of concern for any student of political science.
  • Modern governments are rising more and more as essential instrumentalities of versatile development particularly in the developing nations of Asia etc.
  • We’ve also discussed about the concept of comparative government. the concept of comparative politics, the definitions pertaining to comparative government and politics and the characteristics of comparative government and politics with how it is imperative in the field of political science and the significance of the role of governments in politics.

02

Comparative Government and Politics as a Discipline

Video Part 1 · starts at 11:34

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • At the end of this session, you will be able to define the term political science and its relationship with comparative politics.
  • You’ll also be able to describe how have comparative politics entered the discipline of political science.

Political Science as a Discipline

  • We’re going to discuss about the module one that is subtopic two which pertains to comparative government and politics as a discipline.
  • Now firstly, political science is a very vast domain, a very vast discipline indeed with various subsets like political theory, comparative government in politics, nationalism in India, political thought, but particularly if I speak about comparative government in politics, it cannot be negated from the subject matter of political science, political theory and international relations were such subsets of political science which were very prominent since decades.
  • But as and when society evolved, countries and nation came and nation states came into perspective, comparative government and politics came to be renowned.
  • Because when political systems entered the domain of politics, writers and philosophers raised a point that how would we know that one government is what structure and how’s the other government and how would one government be compared to the other?
  • Just take a hypothetical example that how a democracy would be distinguished from an authoritarian government. In the same context matter, we need something. We need a method.
  • We need a subset to compare and contrast these political activities to compare and contrast these political systems.
  • That is why whenever we read and understand about the subject of comparative government in politics, we always need to understand what is political science.

Political Science & the Study of the State

  • So the term political science is a systematic study of the state and the government. Now state has been in varied shapes and sizes.
  • State is itself an abstract entity and government is a practical necessity.
  • One cannot see where is the state and what about the government.
  • Yes, the government is just a practical necessity because state is just like an invisible god whom we cannot see but is still present.
  • In terms of government, the broader dimension is state and the narrower dimension is the government. So the state functions through the government.
  • The government consists of organs. the government consists of with their structure and its functions.
  • That is why comparative government studies, the organs, the functions and the structures of the government and comparative politics studies the domain and perspectives which are outside this government.

Institutions, Civil Society & Religious Groups

  • That is political institutions, civil society, organization, different religious groups.
  • All of these are paradigms of comparative politics.
  • Political science is also a social science in regard to the practice and theory of politics, the analysis of political systems and the study of political behavior.
  • Political behavior pertinently refers to electoral behavior where you know that why this party is in power. You judge the context.
  • You judge the dimensions as to for how many years they have been po.
  • Why, what, and when are the questions that you answer when you study the political behavior or the electoral behavior of a particular country or a particular political system.

What Is Comparative Government & Politics?

  • Now, what is comparative government in politics?
  • Comparative politics is a study of political understanding of more than one nation, state or country in order to make precise comparisons.
  • Radi on the other hand, comparative government is a subdivision of politics that systematically studies, analyzes and compares the nature of governments and that too in a number of selected countries.
  • So comparative politics is just one or two but comparative government will always take into account numerous and that numerous it depends on what type of countries are you selecting because everything depends on the comparative method. So you need to select the countries.

What Can Be Compared?

  • Well, which one can compare?
  • That is countries should only be compared on one factor or on two factors. But they should be comparable on those factors. You can compare two developed countries.
  • But can you compare a developed country and an underdeveloping country?
  • No, not possible because there will not be comparable aspects. There wouldn’t be comparable factors.

The Comparative Method in Practice

  • That is why both comparative and comparative politics, government and politics study through the comparative method.
  • Now, political science and comparative politics is also a very interesting topic.
  • The study of comparative politics has been primarily concerned thus far with the formal institutions of foreign governments, particularly Western Europe.
  • In this sense, it has been not only limited but also primarily descriptive and formalistic.
  • So when you’re talking about the foreign governments or the formal institutions of Western Europe, you’ll be writing descriptively that is organs would be compared, structures would be compared and their functions would be compared.
  • It will be a formal setup that is what kind of structure is it? What kind of constitution they believe in? Why was the constitution made? What was the context behind it?
  • These are referred to as descriptive studies that is in-depth detail to a certain fact.

Why the Field Is Ill-Defined

  • Its place in the field of political science has been very ill-defined because comparative government is a very respected aspect in political science because you’re comparing governments. you’re paying importance to the institutions.
  • You’re paying importance to a structure and functions.
  • But comparative politics is not looking at those functions at that structure.
  • They are looking at the non-formal institutions.
  • And that is why political science says that it is very ill-defined because you don’t know that which element or which institution which is informal are they speaking about. What are the philosophers speaking about?
  • It was agreed that the general problem of comparison is extraordinarily difficult for political science since it is unlikely that we will ever find two or more societies that are identical in all respects except for a single variable factor.
  • So philosophers have always raised this point that in comparative politics you need to compare.
  • But two countries or two societies looking identical cannot have a single variable factor that can be compared.

The Traditional Method in Political Science

  • That is why comparative politics is ill-defined in political science.
  • Now there is a traditional method in political science that is these approaches were mainly related to the traditional viewpoint of politics which emphasize on the study of the state and government.
  • The characteristics of the traditional approach adopted in political science are they are largely nomative that is they ask the questions like what where who aspects and stresses on the values of politics that is a dominant group dominant then why are they dominant? Are they suppressing the minority in that case?
  • Emphasis of the traditional approach has always been that the study of different political structures.
  • They’ve made very little attempt to relate theory and research and traditional approaches believe that since facts and values are closely interlinked studies in political science can never be scientific because every concept in political science is an overlapping criteria.
  • So there will never be an experimentative method rather always comparative method is used in political science when you deal with a concept of comparative government in politics.
  • Now there are certain defects in the traditional method because as it is it was not appropriate to study comparative government and politics through the traditional method.
  • The term comparative politics is of the recent origin and came into vogue in the 50s of the present century and is indicative of the expanding horizontal political science.
  • The political scientist made a bid to study the political reality through new techniques and approaches.

The Old Concepts in New Light

  • The old concepts were also seen in new light.
  • One of the main reasons which encouraged the development of the new approach for the study of politics was dissatisfaction with the traditional descriptive approach to the subject.
  • So everything was just written down in a descriptive manner whether the structure of the organization whether the function of the institution everything just descriptively written nothing analyzed and compare on parameters.
  • The scholars laid greater emphasis on informal political processes rather than political institutions and state.
  • They borrowed several ideas and concepts from other social sciences and provided the political studies a new empirical orientation.

Comparative Government vs Comparative Politics

  • Now comparative government particularly is little bit different from comparative politics.
  • Like any other form of evolutionary process, comparative government evolves its present over a period.
  • In the evolution of comparative governments, one studies how political systems and procedures vary across countries and across time periods.
  • The actual evidence of undertaking such a study came to prominence in the 1950s, but its roots are even older.
  • Aristotle is always called as the ancestral father of the study of comparative government and politics since the methods that he used in assigning politics among the sciences and problems and questions that he raised are still prevalent in the current political studies.
  • Comparative politics is also an outcome seeking discipline that is wants answers immediately just like a revolutionary.
  • A revolutionary demands solution to a problem immediately while the course of action could be anything.
  • In the same manner, comparative politics is an outcomebased discipline’s actions and outcomes results easily.
  • Comparative politics is emerging as a distinct subdiscipline of political science.
  • It is defined by both substantive and methodological criteria.

Explaining Variation Among Political Units

  • Substantively, research in comparative politics seeks to account for variation among political units on consequential social, political, cultural, and economic outcomes.
  • Comparative politics research places these outcomes on dimensions.
  • For example, a dimension that goes from habesian state of nature to political order and seeks to account for the placement of a political unit in a specific time period on that dimension.
  • It then seeks to account for differences in placement along that dimension among political units and for the same political unit in different time periods. What is comparative government in politics? Political science and comparative politics.
  • The traditional method in political science. the defects of the same method and the concept of comparative government and politics being an outcome seeking discipline.

03

Features and Functions of Comparative Government and Politics

Video Part 1 · starts at 26:34

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • At the end of this session you will be able to define the term politics.
  • You’ll also be able to describe the features and functions of comparative government in politics.

Politics as a Collective Activity

  • Now firstly we always need to understand when we reading about the concept of comparative government in politics that is what is politics?
  • Politics is purely a collective activity involving people who accept a common or at least knowledge a shared fate.
  • Politics presumes an initial diversity of views about goals or means or both.
  • Were we in agreement all the time that politics would be redundant?
  • Politics involves reconciling such differences through discussion and persuasion. Communication is therefore central to politics.

Authoritative Decisions & Binding Agreements

  • Political decisions becomes authoritative.
  • Policy for a group, binding members to agreements that are implemented by force if necessary.
  • Politics costly exists if decisions are reached solely by violence.
  • But force or its threat underpins the execution of policy.
  • Politics in India particularly is about the discussion and negotiation that is held between the bureaucratic members as to what policy should come about for the general public as to what are the terms and conditions that people will be able to aspire for a society.
  • Politics also includes electoral behavior, the political system that one has to adapt to.
  • So all this becomes very new factors in terms of politics and politics itself is a study in political science is very imperative.
  • Every subset of political science, whether it be political theory, comparative government, international relations, all of these subsets involves a term called as politics because politics is not only a discussion or negotiation but also and that is why it can never be redundant.
  • Now characteristics of comparative politics are several but some of them pertains that it focuses on analytical empirical research.
  • It is no longer confined to descriptive studies that is comparative politics is not just that two countries are compared in terms of their political system in terms of their civil society organizations in terms of their political system in terms of their political institutions.

Analytical & Empirical Research

  • It is just not descriptive that what is what and what is not.
  • Analytical empirical research pertains to as to what and why.
  • So what and why will always look into the context into deeper analysis of the context.
  • It seeks to analyze empirically and analytically the actual activities of the governments and their structures and functions.

Scientific Study of What Is & Why

  • It stands for scientific studies of politics.
  • It basically concentrates upon the study of what is and what should be.
  • It is now not confined to the study of formal institutions of government in terms of the legal powers and functions.
  • Comparative politics accepts the desirability and need for adopting an inter interdisciplinary focus.
  • Now moving further to the characteristics of comparative politics.
  • It involves both a comparative study of political structures, functions of national political systems of various states and a comparative study of political structures at work within a single state.
  • The objective of comparative politics is not only to make comparative studies of the only similarities and differences but to build a theory and that theory will always be through a comparative method factors that would be comparable factors that are comparable and that is why its name is self-explanatory comparative politics.
  • It has adopted the concept of system for the study of politics.
  • The concept of political system has virtually replaced the concept of state.

The State as an Abstract Entity

  • State particularly being an abstract entity.
  • An entity that cannot be felt but cannot but cannot be negated either. It can be understood.
  • It can be felt without being visibly present there.

Government as a Component of the State

  • But under the state, we also see the government which becomes an imperative component of the state.
  • Firstly, it is also the characteristics of comparative government leads to essentially being non-comparative.
  • The field of comparative government deals either one country or with parallel descriptions of the institutions of several countries.
  • It is descriptive studies. the descriptions of political institutions is vital for understanding political processes and that it leads to comparative study.
  • It is also parochial accessibility of the country studied, relative ease of overcoming language barriers and the availability of official documents and other source materials as well as cultural affinities account for this fact. It is also considered to be essentially static.
  • The traditional approach has been ignoring the dynam dynamic factors that account for growth and change.

Political Autonomy & the Monographic Approach

  • It is concentrated on what we have as to what we call as political autonomy. It is considered to be monographic as well.
  • That is the most important studies of foreign political systems apart from the basic text have taken the form of monographs that have concentrated on the study of political institutions of one system or on the discussion of a particular institution in different systems.
  • The functions of comparative politics is also very dynamic.
  • The goal of comparative politics is to understand that how political institutions and processes operate by examining their workings across a range of countries.
  • Comparison has many purposes that is even a simple description of political systems and institutions helping us understand the broader context with every work that they do helping thus in turn developing theories and rules of politics.
  • It also investigates how similar problems are approached by different societies and this is a very valuable asset for political science.
  • That is if one country is facing the same problem and the other two is facing the same problem but they will have two different approaches to them.
  • Thus the study of government and politics in different countries designed to better understand them by drawing out the contrast and similarities.

04

Philosophers’ Viewpoints in Comparative Government and Politics

Video Part 1 · starts at 34:53

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • At the end of this session, you will be able to define the concept of comparative politics and you’ll also be able to describe the popular definitions in the domain of comparative politics.

Comparison as the Foundation of the Field

  • Now before moving on to what are the definitions pertaining to comparative politics, it is necessary to understand its main concept.
  • The study of comparative politics involves conscious comparisons in studying political experience, institutions, behavior, processes of the systems of government in a comprehensive manner.
  • It includes the study of extra constitutional agencies.
  • Now in the first module when we discussed about the comparative government I said that comparative government does not deal with extra constitutional agencies which comparative politics does.
  • So an extra constitutional agency would be a civil society organization. It would be an NGO. It would be a ministry in an organization.

Pressure Groups & Other Agencies

  • So all these agencies are never judged or never developed in the concept of comparative government but they are very imperative in the content of comparative politics because they have the immediate connection open or tacit with formal governmental organizations. That is purely it could mean a pressure group.
  • A pressure group does have connotations with the government.
  • They do work from outside the government to pressurize the government to work more to pressurize the government to fulfill the aspirations and desires of the people.
  • But they are somewhere connected to the formal organs and that is why these extra constitutional agencies work in this manner.

Regularities, Similarities & Differences in Political Systems

  • It is therefore concerned with significant regularities, similarities and differences in the working of political institutions and in the patterns of political behavior.
  • Thus, comparative politics involves a comparative study of various political systems either as a whole or through comparative analysis of their structures and functions.
  • Now what is Aristotle views on comparative government in politics?
  • Aristotle has always been considered as the father of comparative government and politics because he was the first philosopher who compared 158 constitution states in terms of their structure, the organ structure, the organs functions and there certain other factors. Yes, absolutely belong to different countries.
  • Aristotle compared various political systems and developed an explanatory theory as to why this structure of monarchy or as to why that particular country is a democracy as to why two countries being communist governments are still separate in terms of certain variables.
  • Aristotle was certainly the first scholar of comparative government and considered the study of comparative government as the oldest and most significant to attract the attention of mankind.
  • Aristotle and other scholars engaged themselves in the comparative investigation of foreign cultures with varying degrees of complexity with it increase in the tension and rivalry between democratic and undemocratic political systems the impact of the so-called third world during the cold war era and the growing importance of informal politics The utility of synthesis of data and the nature and range of comparison underwent a transformation.

Aristotle’s Study of Constitutions

  • The oldest form of comparative government is the study of constitutions which Aristotle has done in great lengths.
  • The first known such work is Aristotle’s compilation of the constitutions and practice of 158 Greek city state of which only the constitution of a survives.
  • So he did compare various constitutions but only the constitution which survived was that of a undoubtedly however comparisons between different city states underpins some of the generalizations in Aristotle’s politics just as comparisons between different living organisms underpin his biological writing.
  • So there are certain generalizations as to if two democratic countries are compared there will also be a general paradigm a general connotation and there will also be a sub subscribed connotation which is at great length it’ll be dealing with descriptive study but at some other variables it’ll just be a little explanation the transformation of comparative government in politics the decreasing emphasis of the traditional approach logically concluded in the so-called behavioral revolution in the 1950s and 1960s.

Transformation of the Study of Comparative Government

  • The study of comparative government was drastically transformed despite consequential reactions against the behavioral tidal wave.
  • It had scaled new heights of precision, firmness and theoretical order.
  • It had also acquired an allgether new style of analysis which was not known till then.
  • Improvement in concepts and methods, impulses coming from interdisciplinary emphasis on area studies and the growing significance of the politics of developing areas all combined to bring about an unadulterated revolution in the study of comparative government.
  • Now there are certain other popular definitions of comparative government and politics.

M. G. Smith, Macridis & Ward on the Field

  • According to MG Smith, comparative politics is a study of the firms of political organs, their properties, their correlation, their variations and modes of change.
  • According to Roy Macridis and Robert Ward, government is not the sole concern of comparative politics.
  • Comparative politics should be concerned with the government structure but at the same time it should take note of the following that is society historical heritage geography and resource endorments its social and economic organizations its ideologies and value systems its parties interest and leadership structure.
  • All of these characteristics are given by McCredius.
  • According to M. Curtis, comparative politics is concerned with significant regularities, similarities and differences in the working of political institutions and political behavior.
  • According to EA free man, comparative politics is comparative analysis of the various forms of government and diverse political institutions.
  • All these definitions provides a basis for the study of comparative governments in its contemporary term.
  • It involves a comparative study of the institutional and mechanistic arrangements along with the empirical and scientific analysis of noninstit of non-institutionalized and non-political determinance of political behavior.
  • According to Jean Blondel, comparative politics is also the study of patents of national governments in the contemporary world.

Eckstein on Aristotle as the Founder

  • Harry Eckstein has rightly observed comparative politics is a particular right to claim Aristotle as an ancestor because the primacy he assigned to politics among the sciences and because the problems he raised and the methods he used are similar to those still current in political studies.
  • As I rightly pointed out earlier that Aristotle has to be considered a very important philosopher in the study of comparative government.
  • Because while studying 158 Greek city states, you recognize what are the variables that are important.
  • What are the political factors to which you’ll be judging two different countries?
  • All these paradigms while become important while studying any countries in relation to comparative government and politics.
  • EA Freeman also stated that comparative politics is comparative analysis of the various forms of government and diverse political institutions.
  • It can be established that comparative politics encompasses a comparative study of not only the institutional and mechanistic arrangements but also an empirical and scientific investigation of non-institutionalized and non-political determinance of political behavior.
  • Empirical study of political processes, structures and functions shape a major part of comparative political studies.
  • It is demonstrated in literature that comparative analysis compares the political systems operating in various societies.
  • For this it considers all three implications of politics that includes political activity, political processes and political power.

Substance of Comparative Politics

  • Now, firstly, circumscribing to the substance of comparative politics.
  • As a subject of study, comparative politics focuses on understanding and explaining political phenomena that takes place within a state, a society, a country and a political system.
  • That is comparative politics will always focus on how a political phenomena is taking place in a particular country. That is just take the example of India.
  • If we are comparing a country like India to a South Asian continent or South Asian regional power that is China, both India and China are considered to be regional powers.
  • Then what are their variables and what are we defining as perspectives?
  • That is how is the state structure of both the countries?

Focus on Internal Political Structure

  • How’s the societal structure? the country in terms of its population, landmark size and the political system.
  • In other words, comparative politics focuses on internal political structures like parliaments and executives, actors like voters, parties and interest groups like policy making, communication, political culture and analyzing them empirically by defining, describing and explaining and predicting their variety that is similarities. and differences across political systems be they national political system, regional, municipal or even supra national political systems.
  • Now studying comparative politics is also very important.
  • Once we know what does comparative politics has a substance in it leading to political systems and other varieties we also need to understand what are we actually studying in it and why are we actually studying it is focused on each country’s internal politics or how governments are structured that is what are the governing institutions and how their functions let’s take the example of India if you’re taking the example of the internal politics of India. Then what is our government structure?
  • That is a parliamentary form of government is the government structure of our country. What are the governing institutions?

Legislature, Executive & Judiciary

  • Institutions like legislature, executive and judiciary.
  • And what are its paradigm functions are what we are actually studying in comparative politics.
  • And if you’re comparing it with another country such as China, we’re going to work in a similar pattern. That is what is a governance structure? It is a communist country.

Governing Institutions

  • What are the governing institutions? That is does it have a legislature? Is its judiciary as strong as India?
  • And how are they actually functioning in terms of the jurisdiction powers?

Government–Population Interaction & Communist States

  • How governments interact with their population and what decisions are made is what the study matter of comparative politics that is India being a welfare state and China being a communist state.
  • The communist state countries are imposing rules and policies on their people.
  • Rather, a country like India follows welfare state that is well-being of the people.
  • How political leaders and population behave in politics and how decisions are made is what is the study of comparative politics.
  • How and who makes influences decisions or policy orientations, leadership and other attributes of political decisions are vital components of comparative politics. The purpose of comparative politics are varied.
  • We widen our understanding of politics in other countries to increase our appreciation of the advantages and disadvantages of our own political system and to enable us to learn from other countries. develop a more sophisticated understanding of politics in general, that is the relationships between governments and peoples and other concepts and processes.
  • Help us understand the linkages between domestic and international affairs.
  • Helps us see the relationship between politics in such fields as science and technology, environment, public health, law, business, religion, ethnicity and culture.
  • Now what are the challenges in the study of comparative politics?
  • The interconnection between norms, institutions and behavior is the first challenge.

Natural vs Imposed Governments

  • This aspect stems from the fact that some governments exist naturally and others are imposed.
  • This was examined through the study of gap between constitutions and real political life as no constitution will ever be fully implemented.
  • Constitution makers of the 18th and 19th centuries attempted to modify societies in a certain way as they tried to impose rules.
  • Constitutional rule is therefore a form of imposed system of government.
  • The distinction between natural and imposed arrangements is a problem for political systems. The second challenge is a range of variables.
  • The analysis can become precise only when it is possible to list and weigh the numerous variables which enter the definition of a political system.
  • The list of variables is impressive but the task impossible to calculate as many of these variables lack quantitative formalization.
  • Economic conditions, social conditions, the climate, the physical geography and some others all seem to be part of the explanation of political system and all have been used at one period or another by political scientists anxious to explain the norms institution and behaviors of nations.
  • Since the range of variables of politics is very large, it is not possible to empirically and comprehensively analyze all of these.

Paucity of Information

  • The third challenge is a paucity of information.
  • cross-national analysis is made particularly difficult between several countries where the system is imposed and often this information is lagging like a country like North Korea. Certain information cannot be reached out. So there’ll be paucity of information.
  • Totalitarian countries refuse to give access to information.
  • Admittedly, even the most open country does operate limited and indirect censorship.
  • In many circumstances, lack of information possess a serious hindrance in the way of comparative politics.
  • Many governments are not willing to let the political scientists have a look into their records and files.

Problems of Using the Scientific Method

  • The next challenge is a problems in the way of using scientific method and also problems of comparative politics.
  • The problem resulting from complexity of social data. The problem of using empirical methods. Problem verification and prediction. Explanation and prediction. The problem arising from its dynamic nature. And the problem of applying scientific method.

Problems of Empirical Study & Objectivity

  • The problem of empirical study is another one.
  • Comparative politics stands for scientific and empirical study of all phenomena of politics.
  • It gives rise to the problems associated with the observation and collection of facts.
  • This problem becomes bigger when one is to collect facts about the operation of authoritarian and totalitarian political systems. The dynamic nature of politics.
  • Politics is an aspect of human behavior and like all other aspects of human behavior, it is also highly dynamic.
  • Further, a g big gap is always present in the theory and practice of all political systems.
  • This always acts as a big hindrance to the way of every realistic study of politics. The problem of objectivity.
  • Scientific and empirical study of politics demands objectivity in the observation.
  • It demands an ability to keep values and biases away.
  • The researcher must maintain a high level of alertness.

Hindrances to Empirical Study

  • The requirement also acts as a source of big hindrance.
  • Thus there have been present several problems and hindrances in the way of comparative political studies.
  • But political scientists gradually need to overcome this step by step.
  • Why studying comparative politics is necessary the challenges in the study of comparative politics.

08

Role of Comparative Government and Politics in the Contemporary Scenario

Video Part 1 · starts at 1:14:23

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • That is at the end of this session, you will be able to define the old intellectual tradition of comparative politics.
  • You’ll also be able to describe new elements and innovations in comparative politics.

The Learned Tradition of Comparative Politics

  • But firstly, what is a learned tradition of comparative politics?
  • It is very important to understand comparative government looks at the perspectives of government.
  • The functioning of comparative government that is the legislature organ, the executive organ and the judicial organs.
  • But comparative politics will also be viewing other institutions like a civil society organization, like an NGO.
  • So the learned tradition of comparative politics encompasses the scope of comparative politics was very limited and parochial.
  • It was confined to the study of only constitutions which Aristotle did.
  • He did study the 158 Greek city state constitutions and political institutions in respect of their features, powers and positions.
  • It was parochial in the sense that it involved a study of only European constitutions and amongst the European constitutions the British constitution was regarded as the mother of all constitutions as it is a standard constitutions measuring the worth of all other constitution.
  • The emphasis was on the study of government and institution.

Parochialism: Macridis’s Observation

  • As R. C. Macridis has observed that the study of governments were more concerned with studying foreign governments than with comparative politics.
  • Now let the contemporary vision of comparative politics is very distinct and dynamic in contemporary times.
  • Comparative politics has come out of its parochialism and limitedness.
  • So it’s not only viewing constitutions the organs of the government but it has started to move beyond it has come to acquire a very wide scope which includes the analysis and comparison of political processes political activities its structures and functions in all the continents such as Europe Asian Africa and Latin America.
  • After the second world war, it was undergone revolutionary changes in respect of its scope and methodology.
  • The shortcomings of the traditional studies led to eliminate formalistic legal institutionalism, crude empiricism and normativism in comparative politics.

The Global Purview Today

  • Today it includes all that comes within the purview of politics.
  • That is every part of the globe is included here.
  • Today comparative politics includes all that comes within the purview of politics.
  • It includes the study of all political processes and power relations found in every part of the globe. Comparative politics.
  • The elements of contemporary comparative politics are as follows. It includes all political structures.
  • Comparative politics includes the study of all structures formal and informal, governmental and extragovernmental.

Beyond the Three Organs of Government

  • Extragovernmental are interest groups, pressure groups directly or indirectly involved in the government processes.
  • It is not confined to the study of three organs, just the legislature, executive and judiciary.
  • Along with these bureaucracy, pressure groups, interest groups, elite groups, political parties all have acquired the new dimension of comparative politics. The study of political behavior.
  • Another important part of comparative politics is the study of the scope of actual behavior of the people in the processes of politics.
  • Voting behavior, political participation, leadership, recruitment, elite behavior, mass politics, populism form integral part of the study of comparative politics.
  • The functional studies is an important element in the contemporary comparative politics.
  • Comparative politics seeks to study politics less from the point of view of the legal institutions in terms of their powers and more from the point of view of the functions which constitute the political processes and the actual operation in the environment.

Interest Articulation, Aggregation & Political Communication

  • It studies the functions of interest articulation, interest aggregation, political communication, rule making, rule applications, education, socialization, policymaking, environmental studies.
  • The study of politics demands the study of psychological, sociological, political and anthropological environments.
  • For studying this, the political scientists have developed concepts like political culture, political socialization and political modernization.
  • The study of political culture of various political systems forms a very peculiar element in comparative politics.
  • The concept has enhanced the ability of political scientists and has helped them to analyze the difference in the working of similar political systems.
  • The next element is assert of more comprehensive scope to break out of parochialism and ethnocentrism.
  • These are the new innovations in comparative politics to give equal importance to the study of non-western governments as well.
  • To make compar comparative political studies comprehensive, past as well as present political systems is to be studied which forms a huge component in comparative politics.
  • There should also be search for realism that is this includes under the political system at what it is.

Dynamic Forces of Politics

  • It includes examination of structures and processes involved in politics and policy making study of governmental processes namely rule making and rule application.
  • Its emphasis is on the study of dynamic forces of politics.
  • It involves the techniques of behavioral studies. There also has to be the search for precision.
  • Political science always involves precise outcomes of political phenomena.
  • Many political scientists have produced studies of electoral trends based in voting statistics, studies of factors affecting voters choices based upon samples, survey studies correlating quantitative social data and the characteristics of political system.
  • All the attempts have been directed in the direction of securing position in comparative political studies.

The Search for a New Intellectual Order

  • The search for new intellectual order.
  • The above three tendencies have trained the traditional theoretical framework in conceptual vocabularies.
  • Concepts such as state, constitution, presentation, rights, duties cannot codify such activities as extra constitutional.
  • New concepts such as political culture, political elite have become popular.
  • Thus, the above four directions have greatly revolutionized the contemporary comparative political studies.
  • These point are towards the building of a science of politics and comparative politics.

Who Broadened the Scope of Comparative Government?

  • That is it encompasses the similarities between comparative government and politics.
  • Now who broaden the scope of comparative government?
  • Comparative government came initially developed and established in the field of political science as a very small subset.
  • It was just a study first by Aristotle who’s also known as a father of comparative government in politics.
  • But who actually broadened the scope is quite difficult to understand and assimilate.
  • The study of comparative government in politics in its latest form includes significant contributions of those recent writers who’ve broadened its scope by taking into the areas of study of more and more countries particularly the Afroasian and Latin American regions better known as a world of developing areas. Even India is a developing nation.
  • These writers have paid their sincere heed to the council of Lord James Bryce who once said that the time seems to have arrived when the actualities of government in its various forms should be investigated.
  • The eminent writers in comparative politics have not only endorsed but also improved upon the observations of James D. shocked well that as we pass from France to Italy, Switzerland, Germany and USSR, there is no common thread, no criteria of why these particular countries were selected and no examination of the factors that account for similarities and diversities. The concept of comparative politics.
  • Comparative politics is a broader scope to cover all that comes within the purview of comparative government and in addition to that all else that may be designed to the study of non-state politics that is which includes non-state actors as well the role of the functionaries of interest group the role of the functionaries of pressure groups all this is covered according to Curtis comparative politics is concerned with significant regularities, similarities and differences in the working of political institutions and in political behavior.

Curtis on Similarities & Differences

  • Curtis, however, makes it obvious that the inquiry into similarities and differences is not a search for certainty or predictability.
  • Nor does it start from the premise that what is not scientific is not knowledge.
  • System classification and categories are always tentative. They cannot claim finality.

Beyond Involuntary Responses to Stimuli

  • Politics can be reduced to a series of involuntary and automatic responses to stimuli.
  • But the concept of comparative government, comparative government covers a comparative study of different political systems, special emphasis on the institutions and functions.
  • As Sidney Verba consistently suggests, look beyond the description to more theoretically relevant problems. look beyond formal institutions to political processes and political functions to look beyond the countries of Western Europe that is look to the Asian continent as well to the new nations of Asia, Africa and Latin America.
  • According to Turkote, comparative government usually refers to the study of institutions and functions of country or nation states in Europe with attention to the executives, legislature and judiciaries as well as such supplementary organizations as political parties and pressure groups.
  • In other words, the scope of comparative politics is wider than that of compive government.
  • Because if you look at the case of India comparative government, we’ll only be studying about the organs that is the legislature, executive, judiciary and media.
  • But if we speak about comparative politics, then it will go beyond that.
  • That is it will be studying about the civil society organization, the NOS’s, the all these groups, the majoritarian groups, the minoritarian groups.
  • It will it will attach all these groups in its circumcription.

Vertical & Horizontal Comparison

  • Now the comparative government versus comparative politics.
  • Several political scientists have pointed out that the term comparative politics prefer to the term comparative government as a scope of the former is wider more comprehensive to include all essential characteristics that we have that we discussed in the last slide.
  • One may agree with the observation of Blondel that the term comparative government has two aspects horizontal and vertical and this term may be identified with comparative politics if both the aspects are considered.
  • Blunder may be justified to some extent in saying comparative government becomes comparative politics when both vertical and horizontal aspects of comparisons are considered that lead to this definition.

Edward Freeman’s Viewpoint

  • Comparative government can thus be defined in a preliminary fashion as a study of patterns of national government in the contemporary world.
  • Edward Freeman viewpoint is very essential to understand both these disciplines.
  • Edward Freeman tries to bring out a distinction between these two words.
  • By comparative government, he means a comparative study of political institutions of forms of government. under the name of comparative politics.
  • He wishes to point out and bring together many analogies which are to be seen between the political institutions of times and countries most remote from one another.
  • Comparative politics though concerned with significant regularities, similarities and differences in the sphere of comparative political institutions and human behaviors.
  • The work of comparison should neither be done half-heartedly to ignore what much is useful nor should it be taken to the extremes of oversimplifying anything.
  • So there’s a very thin line between of different comparative government and politics and some political scientists have always also used this simultaneously with each other but some scientists have uncovered it as two different paradigms.
  • But if we take in the terms of the Asian continent, comparative government and both comparative politics as a study has already been undertaken.

10

Differences Between Comparative Government and Comparative Politics

Video Part 1 · starts at 1:31:11

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • At the end of this session, you will be able to define the concept of political system.
  • You’ll also be able to describe the distinction between comparative government and politics.

Institutions vs Politics

  • Now, as I said, the difference between comparative government and politics is not that huge.
  • That means as comparative government mainly deals with institutions like the legislature, the executive and the judiciary and other organs institutions.
  • But comparative politics particularly deals with other institutions such as the civil society organizations, the NOS’s, the international organizations but not governments.
  • It deals with political systems that is politics.
  • Thus there’s a very thin line of difference between comparative government and politics. Now circumscribing to what is political system.
  • The difference between comparative politics and comparative government is very subtle and by various philosophers they are referred to as one.
  • So studying the political system in terms of its divisions, countries and regions has had substantial history.

Politics as Practice & Theory of Governance

  • Politics is a practice and theory related to governance.
  • Organized control of human community which also describe the practice of distribution of power not only with a particular community but also with interrelating communities.
  • Furthermore, a political system is a framework of practices within a particular political territory.
  • If I talk about the case of India, India has a huge landmass with huge diversity.
  • Despite huge diversity, we have unity that is unity in diversity.
  • But we live in a particular territory with different ethnicities.

Political Systems Differ Between Countries

  • The political system of one country may or sometimes may not be different from that of another country or territory.
  • The body within the political power of a particular territory is called a government.
  • Thus political system is different but somewhere in consonance with a government and government in entirety is totally different.
  • But government comes under the bigger paradigm of state.
  • State is an abstract entity which just cannot be seen but it is present and within the state we have the government through which comparativist deal with.

Meaning of Comparative Politics

  • Now what is comparative politics?
  • It’s a further understanding of comparative politics from its basic meaning.
  • Comparative politics is a term referring to the study of political understanding of nation state or country in order to make precise comparisons.
  • It is an area of study in politics that is largely discussed and studied all over the world.
  • There are basically two approaches to the study of comparative politics that is cross-national approach and the other being the area studies approach.
  • There are many people in this world who take up their doctorate thesis on the area studies.

Cross-National Approaches

  • cross-national approaches when two nation state are actually compared in terms of their political system.
  • The first type of approach involves simultaneous studying of many nation state in order to obtain a wider understanding of theories and their applications.
  • The latter type of approach that is the area studies approach deals with an in-depth analysis of politics within a particular political territory, a state, a country, a nation state or a region of the world. What is comparative government?
  • Compitive government is a subdivision of politics that systematically analyzes and compares the nature of governments in several selected countries.
  • A government is a topmost hierarch hierarchical body of governance in the country or a nation state.

Hierarchy & the Bureaucratic Machinery

  • Why is it called as hierarchy? Because it has a bureaucratic machinery. There will be an MP.
  • There will be a member of parliament, an MLA and sub down there, under secretaries, secretaries, directors.
  • All of this circumscribes to a hierarchical body of governance which is in entirety called as a bureaucratic machinery.

Forms of Government Studied & Analysed

  • Through the study of comparative government, different forms of government in are encountered across the world.
  • How are they done? that is they are studied, analyzed and then compared.
  • It is done with a view to understand the differences and seek any potential practices a country can learn from one another and adapt.
  • Now there’s a huge difference between comparative government and politics.
  • Comparative politics is a wider body whereas comparative government has various subdivisions.
  • Comparative politics studies and compares different theories and political practices of countries and nation states.
  • Comparative government is a study, analysis and comparison of different government systems around the world.
  • Comparative politics is not only about the government.
  • It encompasses studying political aspects in terms of governance, foreign policies, etc.
  • However, comparative government only compares different forms of governmental bodies in the world.

Formal Institutions in Comparative Government

  • Comparative government is concerned with the study of formal political institutions like executive, judiciary and bureaucracy alone in comparative politics.
  • The other factors that is the influence of the working of political institutions are also considered.
  • In other words, political comparative politics makes a study of the formal as well as informal political institutions.
  • Thus, the scope of comparative politics is wider than that of comparative government despite such making comparison which is central to the study of both.
  • Comparative government was chiefly confined to the study of the political institutions or western democratic countries.
  • Comparative politics concentrates in the study of political institutions of all the countries of the world.
  • It has laid special emphasis on the study of political institutions of the states which have emerged in the 20th century.
  • Comparative government involves only descriptive study of the political institutions and makes only formal study of the political institutions provided by the constitutions.

Analytical Study in Comparative Politics

  • While in the comparative politics concentrates in analytical study of the various political institutions.
  • Investigation and experimentation constitute prominent features of comparative politics.
  • Comparative government concern itself only with the political activities of political institutions while comparative politics also considers economic the cultural and social factors.
  • Thus it tries to examine the political institutions through an interdisciplinary approach.

Origin of Comparative Politics

Video Part 1 · starts at 1:40:58

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • Firstly, at the end of this session, you will be able to define the phases of comparative politics.
  • You’ll also be able to describe the characteristics of the third phase of comparative politics.

Phases of Comparative Politics

  • Now firstly there are phases is comparison to what is comparative politics.
  • Comparative politics did not come in one go but it developed in different phases.
  • The first phase being the 1950s phase of comparative politics.
  • The study of comparative politics became highly significant in the 1950s when good number of leading American political scientists sought to transform politics by taking the study of this subject from foreign to comparative political phenomena and from the study of governments to the study of political systems.
  • In broad terms, the transformation which has taken place has been from a field which would most appropriately be labeled as foreign governments to one which most adequately be called comparative political systems.
  • However, the historical development of this subject may be roughly put into three phases. that is the unsophisticated phase being the 1950 phase itself.
  • The sophisticated phase and the increasingly sophisticated phase.
  • Now further the contributions made to the study of politics by great figures that is Aristotle, Machiavelli, de Tocqueville, Bryce, Ostrogorski and Weber belong to the first phase who simply believe the comparative method for the primary purpose of understanding better political organizations.

Aristotle & the Early Comparative Method

  • And we all understand that Aristotle is considered to be the father of comparative politics and also he developed a theory of 158 Greek states comparing them to vast factors.
  • These writers employed what was called the comparative method that aimed through the study of existing politics or those which had existed in the past to assemble a definite body of material from which the investigator by selection compare and eliminate may discover the ideal types and progressive forces of political history.
  • John Stuart Mill too undertook to show that the comparative method may assume several forms.
  • The most perfect of which is a process of difference by which two politics identical in every particular except one are compared with a view to discovering the effect of the differing factor.
  • Now we come to the second phase of comparative politics.
  • The contributions of some important recent writers like Samuel Huntington, Mhas Pulam and Roy C. Macridis may be included in the second phase who made to use the comparative method.
  • The writers belonging to this category applied the instruments of institutional comparisons in a quite rigorous manner to present a better in the sense realistic study of the governments what they desire to address his political systems.
  • This is called as a sophisticated phase in the growth of the subjective comparative politics.
  • In as much as these writers were concerned with various strategies of comparison, area studies, configurative approach, institutional functional comparisons, a problem based orientation and with various methodological problems.

Third Phase: Easton, Input & Feedback

  • Now the third phase of comparative politics the contributors include David Easton who’s also talked about the general systems theory with an input and an output model and a feedback mechanism that is inputs. Policies will be made for the people.
  • People in turn will provide inputs on those policies and then those policies people where they can provide a feedback.
  • Other contributors of this phase include Almond, James C. Coleman, Karl Deutsch, Harold Lasswell, Robert Dahl, Edward Shils, Harry Eckstein, David Apter, Lucian Pye.
  • All of these people are included in the final phase.
  • It may rightly be described as a mark of an increasingly sophisticated phase in the growth of comparative politics.
  • So others being still sophisticated but in a more lesser manner.

Interrelated Concepts: Inputs, Outputs & Demands

  • The writers belonging to this phase made use of interrelated set of concepts which one can also refer to as interdisciplinary concepts for the sake of presenting their contributions based in comparative analysis though they are provided a specialized vocabulary in their own ways.
  • As Robert says, if eastern talks of inputs, outputs, demands, gatekeepers, feedback, supports and stresses environment, political authorities, then almond offers a set of input and output functions.
  • An almond does not give us the approach of feedback mechanisms.
  • Now the main characteristics of the third phase of comparative politics includes the analytical and the empirical investigation.
  • The analytical come empirical investigation adopted by the writers belong to the latest phase has enlarged the field of our inquiry.

Eckstein on the Late 19th Century

  • It has cleared up the mist in which many helpful distinctions within the framework of political studies lay abscued.
  • Eckstein referred to the late decades of the 19th century as a period in which political science influenced by prim primitive positivism affected a divorce between its normative and descriptive concerns.
  • He further says that in the realm of comparative government more and more writers turn from a concern for the evaluation of governmental firms to a pure description. study.
  • The second characteristics is a study of the infrastructure.
  • The study of comparative politics is not confined to the formal structures of government as well as with the trend of traditional political scientist.
  • The role of political parties, pressure groups such as become significant as the role of legislature and executive in the study of modern political systems.
  • As Blondel says, structures of government do exist and they must exist because this is a way in which tension is reduced and delayed and thereby tension decreases and the polity is maintained but structures change gradually and in a complex fashion.
  • The third characteristics is the emphasis on the study of developing societies.

The Subject-Matter Realisation

  • What has added more to the significance of the study of comparative politics is the emphasis of more writers on the politics of developing areas.
  • It has occurred as a result of the realization that the subjective comparative politics must include all governments along with the infrastructures that exist in the contemporary world and where possible references to governments throughout time.
  • The study of comparative governments is no longer a study of the selected European and American governments.
  • It is as much a study of developed western governments as those of the developing political systems of the poor and backward countries of the Afro-Asian and Latin American world.
  • The fourth characteristics is the interdisciplinary approach.

Value-Free Political Theory

  • What has really enriched the field of comparative politics and also a complex subject is the focus of interdisciplinary writers have made more and more use of tools that they borrowed from the disciplines of sociology, psychology, economics, anthropology and natural sciences like biology.
  • Another characteristics is a value-free political theory.
  • The subjective political science has lost its normative aspect and assumed empirical dimension in the sphere of comparative politics.
  • The result is that value free political theory has replaced value laden political theory.
  • The term value is used by writers in comparative politics in the sense of a price or worth that a thing gets after it is recognized by the policy maker.
  • There’s no value in a thing unless it is allocated by those who are in authority.
  • The second phase and the third phase of comparative politics which is the most sophisticated one and the characteristics of the third phase of comparative politics itself.

Differing Interpretations on the Origin of the Comparative Study of Politics

Video Part 1 · starts at 1:52:24

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • At the end of this session, you will be able to define the origin of comparative politics.
  • You’ll also be able to describe two phases of origin of comparative politics.

Two Revolutions in the Evolution of the Field

  • Now firstly, it is imperative to understand the origin of comparative politics.
  • The evolution of comparative politics is seen as punctuated by two revolutions.
  • Firstly, the behavioral revolution during the immediate postworld war II years until the mid the second scientific revolution which started out around the end of the cold war and is still ongoing.
  • On both occasions, the impetus for change came from developments in the field of American politics and was justified in the name of science.
  • However, the ideas advocated by these two revolutions differed.
  • The behavioral revolution drew heavily on sociology.
  • In contrast, the second scientific revolution imported many ideas from economics and put a heavier emphasis on methodology.
  • Now, what is the origin of behavioral revolution?

The First Turning Point

  • The first turning point in the evolution of political science can be conveniently dated to the publication of a manifesto for a new science of politics which implied a departure from historical approach embraced by many of the founders of political science.
  • One reason why the impetus for a new approach to political science was temporarily muted was that it was centered in but also restricted to the study of American politics.
  • Initially political science was conceived as practically synonymous with the study of comparative politics or as it was usually called in those days comparative government.
  • Burgess and other founders of political science was strong proponents of historical comparative method.
  • As the boundaries between political science and other disciplines were settled, another processes of differentiation leading to the formation of fields within political science began to unfold. Now what is a concept of behavioralism?
  • Behavioralist in comparative politics stood for two distinct ideas. one concerned with a proper subject matter of comparative politics.

Behavioralist Reaction

  • And in this regard, behavioralists reacted against the definition of the field that restricted its scope to the formal institutions of government and sought to include a range of informal procedures and behaviors.
  • A second key idea was the need for a scientific approach to theory and methods.
  • Behavioralists were opposed to what they saw as vague ratified theory and a theoretical empirics and argued for systematic theory and empirical testing.
  • Thus, behaviorists sought to bring about major changes in the established practices of comparative politics.
  • Now, what is the influence of theories in comparative politics through behavioralism?
  • Behavioralism, broadening of the field scope beyond government and formal institutions, open comparative politics to a range of theoretical influences from other disciplines. The strongest influence was sociology.
  • Indeed, vearian parsonian concepts played a central role in structural functionalism. That is Parsons in 1951.
  • The dominant meta theory of the time and some of the most influential contributions to comparative politics were written by scholars trained as sociologist.

Anthropology, Psychology & Structural Functionalism

  • moreover, anthropology had some influence in structural functionalism as did social psychology on the literature on political culture by Almond and Verba 1963.
  • Thus behavioralist helped political science overcome its earlier isolation from other social sciences and this reconnection to other disciplines was associated with a solitary emphasis on theorizing. Now what is a postbehavioral period?
  • The ascendancy of behavioralism in comparative politics came to an end in mid 1960s.
  • Critics of behavioralism started earlier in the mid 1950s and behavioral work continued after 1966.

Culmination & Decline of the Behavioral Revolution

  • moreover, elaborate metatheoretical formulations by leading voices of the behavioral revolution were published in 1965 and 1966.
  • But these works signal the culmination and decline of a research program rather than as a serving as a spur to further research.
  • Indeed, the initiative quickly shifted away from the system builders who had taken the lead in elaborating structural functionalism as a general theory of politics.
  • Now the second scientific revolution stood from 1989 to present.
  • A new phase in the evolution of comparative politics began with a push to more scientific constituted in 1989 with the aim of counteracting the fragmentation of the field induced by area studies and focus of much research.
  • This emphasis in science of course was reminiscent of the behavioral revolution. and statements about the limitations of area studies research even echoed calls made by behavioralist.
  • moreover, as has been the case with the behavioral revolution, this second scientific evolution in comparative politics was not homegrown, but rather the product of the importation of ideas that had already been hatched and elaborated in the field of American politics.
  • Thus whenever you’re speaking about the origin of comparative politics these two ideas that is a behavioral revolution and the scientific revolution takes an important center stage.
  • Science or the age of reasoning as it was also called with the subject matter of comparative politics.
  • behavioralism has also been launched by David Non as a very famous political science philosopher.

Globalisation & the Nation-State
