  • But when you talk about globalizations in fact then a country which is a superpower a country which is as developed as yours will also be compared because probably they might be bigger in population but their defense might be very strong.
  • You might be smaller in population but your defense is weak.
  • So why arises the question in case when we discuss about impact of globalization on comparative politics.
  • You’ll also be able to describe the impact of globalization on comparative politics.
  • Now firstly we need to understand to know its impact that what is globalization.

Rebirth of History & Identity Issues

  • In the last decade of the 20th century the world saw the rebirth of history and political death of territoriality and national identity.
  • That means once you globalize you will always have the problems of territory.
  • That is you are in this particular continent but you’ll also be influenced by other continents because you’re globalized. You’re no more a localized country.
  • Your national identity because there will be people migrating to your country.
  • Your people might be migrating to the other country. So the scenario of national identity. Who are you? Francis Fukuyama.
  • The United States regards it as a universalization of western liberal democracy as a final form of human government.

Friedman on Globalization

  • An American columnist Thomas Friedman identifies it with integration of trade, finance and information that is creating a single global market and culture.
  • It simply means that now what is local is global and vice versa.
  • So when you’re reading this line as to what is local is now global. This happened since the time of 1991.
  • But if I talk about after the time of covid pandemic in the contemporary scenario countries are moving again towards local and no more global because the country’s economy have decreased a lot.
  • So that is why to fix their economies they need to focus more on local instead of global.

Giddens’s Definition & Dimensions of Globalization

  • Thus, Anthony Giddens says, "Globalization is the intensification of worldwide social relations which links distant localities in such a way that local happenings are shaped by events occurring many miles away and vice versa.
  • Now moving ahead to the dimensions of globalization.
  • The first dimension includes the social cultural dimension of globalization.
  • It has its definite impact on the social and cultural life of the people.
  • That is when we when India as a country globalized, we shifted from our local culture to the western accessories.
  • That is the culture of jeans, the culture of wearing western clothes.
  • So we shifted from our traditional culture to the western country’s culture and that is the biggest impact of globalization on the social culture.
  • How we live that is we always had a we always as a country had a communistic living.
  • So people started to live as individuals which is very prominent in the United States economy in the western country’s economy.

Weakening Institutions & New Identities

  • Traditional institutions are growing weak and new identities are emerging that do not belong to any community or nation.
  • New developments in the field of IT transform the cultural patterns of a people’s life.
  • Traditional institutions are growing weak and new identities are emerging that do not belong to any community or nation.
  • New developments in the field of information technology transform the cultural pattern of a people’s life of an individual’s life.
  • It appears that a global society is emerging built on shared values and ideals that poses a serious challenge to the fanatics of swadeshi.
  • The next dimension being the economic dimension of globalization.
  • It is very much visible in the sphere of markets, trade, goods and financial investments.
  • It also extends to flow of services, technology, information and ideas across national boundaries.
  • Globalization has its natural linkage with liberalization. So what is globalization? Liberalization how they’re linked together.

India’s Liberalisation & the Open Economy

  • So first India was liberalized.
  • That is its economy was open to other countries so that they could set up their factories, their companies, their customer care centers in India which had a reasonable labor market.
  • This allowed India’s economy to grow as well because capital was flowing and multinational companies as we term them and corporations were spreading their network across the countries of the world including India.
  • Foreign direct investment became a worldwide phenomenon.

Political Dimension of Globalization

  • The next dimension being the political dimension of globalization.
  • The political aspect of globalization has an importance of its own because it affects the nation state system that has had its long history since the west failure treaty of 1648.
  • It has affected the external aspect of sovereignty and entailed the end of the welfare state.
  • It is creating a new model of state that acts in collaboration with several non-state actors.
  • The borders of the states have become outdated on account of the assault of information technology.
  • Television network has demolished the iron curtain and the bamboo curtain.

Features of Globalization & Their Impact

  • Now moving to the features of globalization which tells us how did it actually impact comparative politics.
  • Many things happens in contemporary world largely respective of territorial distances and borders.
  • It involves a complex mix of concurrent tendency towards cultural convergence on one hand and an increase in groups differentiation on the other. It has not brought about the end of geography.
  • It has created a new super territorial sphere alongside and interrelated with old territorial geography within globalization and social relations. acquire a host of non-territorial qualities.
  • Crucial conditions for effective sovereignty are removed.
  • System is dissolved in a delude of electronic and other flows.
  • Now what is the impact of globalization on comparative politics?

The Sovereign State & Restricted Autonomy

  • Modern state is regarded as a sovereign entity which according to the interpretation of Hobbes, Austin and Hegel is omnipotent.
  • The autonomy of the states has been restricted to a considerable extent.
  • They’re to follow the directives of international bodies like the World Trade Organization, the International Monetary Fund, the World Bank and the like.
  • The nation state is still a sovereign entity, but its sovereignty stands eroded to a certain extent that is identified with their loss of control.
  • In a true sense, it is not a diminution of legal and actual control over the process of determining policy directions, but rather a diminish of the capacity to achieve these policies once they’ve been set.

The Contracted State in the Economic Sphere

  • In the economic sphere, globalization has created the model of a contracted and atrophied state.
  • The growing trend of globalization has strengthened the case of new social movements which desire to establish a civil society in the countries of the world where it is non-existent.
  • The movements of the human right activists, the environmentalist and the feminist may be referred to at this stage.
  • Globalization is creating a transnational state with transnational citizenship.
  • As a people once stop move and settle down in some other country as citizens in the words of Will Kymlicka a kind of multicultural citizenship or also multiculturalism comes up.
  • But this session purely discussed about the impact of globalization which is very imperative not only to the core of comparative politics in India but to the core of the discipline of comparative politics.

A Major Lacuna & Third World Scholars

Nature, Scope, Methods & Approaches

15

Scope of Comparative Politics

Video Part 1 · starts at 2:26:35

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • At the end of this session you will be able to define the concept of comparative politics and we will also discuss the scope of comparative politics.

Starting with the Scope

  • So we’ll study in detail about comparative government and politics.
  • So we’ll start our first presentation with the scope of comparative government and politics.
  • Then only we can understand the scope of comparative politics.
  • So as we know that the study of comparative politics which involves conscious comparison in the studying political science experiences various institutions which are involved in the political system behavior and processes of the system of government in a comprehensive manner.
  • So it includes the study of even extra constitutional agencies having their immediate connection open or tactic with formal governmental organs.
  • So it is therefore concerned with the significant regularities, similarities and differences in the working of political institutions and in the pattern of political behavior.

Comparative Study of Political Systems

  • In simple words, we can say comparative politics involves a comparative study of various political system either as a whole or through a comparative analysis of their structures and functions.
  • There are some popular definitions of comparative politics through which you can understand comparative politics in detail.
  • According to MJ Smith, comparative politics is the study of the forms of related to the political organization, their properties, correlations, variations and mode of change.
  • So we can say that government is not the sole concern of students of comparative politics.

Regularities, Similarities & Differences

Nature of Comparative Politics

Meaning Before Nature

  • But as we know that before discussing the nature of comparative politics, we will focus on the meaning and definition of comparative politics and we’ll try to understand what is all about comparative politics.
  • So as you can understand with the name of comparative political system it means it talks about the comparison related to the political system.
  • So initially we were focusing more on the political system, its growth, its better institutionalization, its cooperation with people.
  • But later we realized that we need to compare also we need to see the differences and similarity among the nations and their structures.
  • So we realize that we need to have a kind of comparison in the political system to bring change for our growth for improvement in a political system and we focused more on the comparative study.
  • So in this manner we can say that the study of comparative politics which involves conscious comparison in studying political experiences, institutions, behaviors and processes of the system of government.
  • So in this manner we can say that we talk about the comprehensive study or differences or similarity related to the institutional structures and behavior of the political system and which include the extra constitutional agencies whether it is related to the political system or non-political agencies as well.
  • So therefore we can say that comparative politics is concerned with significant regularities and similarities with certain differences in the working of political institutions and in the patterns of political behavior.
  • So in simple words we can say that comparative politics involves a comparative study of various political system either as a whole or through a comparative analysis of a particular structures or certain functions.

Comparative Politics as Old as Political Science

  • So comparative politics is as old as political science.
  • Aristotle the father of political science used comparative methods for comprehending and analyzing principles, issues and problems of the Greek city states.
  • He used the knowledge gained for building his theory of politics.
  • Following Aristotle, several political thinkers began using the comparative method for analyzing and presenting their views and conclusion about politics.
  • Thus, it can be legitimately observed that comparative politics had its own origin with Aristotle.

Comparative Politics as an Autonomous Dimension

  • In the contemporary times, comparative politics stand recognized as a primary and essential dimension of the study of political system.
  • A large number of political scientists even regarded it as an autonomous discipline because it is a vast scope and importance for a comprehensive understanding of politics in all the societies and nations.
  • So we can say that there are comparative approach which has emerged as a very useful and highly popular approach for the study of political system.
  • So comparative study of political system forms an integral part of the study of politics.
  • And now we will focus on the nature of comparative politics.
  • As we have discussed a lot about the meaning and definition of comparative politics.
  • Now we can understand the nature and its behavior as a subject or as a discipline.
  • So the nature and the scope of comparative politics can only be understood by understanding the main features and importance of comparative political system in two terms comparative politics and comparative government.

Institutions & Legal Powers

  • So we can focus on both the areas where we can talk about comparative politics which includes each and everything which is related to the political system and another thing is the comparative government which talks about the three major organs of the government which are used lightly and interchangeably but there are differences as well.
  • So comparative politics includes a study of characteristics and legal powers of the political system which exist in different states.
  • So it is a study of states and other political institutions in terms of legal authority, function and position on a comparative basis.
  • So the study of comparative politics involves careful comparison in the studying of actions and processes of a major governmental systems.
  • Therefore, we can say that comparative politics can be defined as a subject comparing the political systems of different parts of the world with the aim of understanding and defining the essence of politics and devising scientific theories of politics as well.
  • So comparative politics can be established to include comparative studies of empirical and scientific research on the institutional and nonpolitical determinants of political behavior as well as institutional and mechanical arrangements.
  • Empirical studies of political process, structures and functions form a major part of comparative politics.
  • So comparative politics has emerged as a new science of politics.
  • It prohibits the non-inclusive scope, formal character, legal and institutionalized framework, normative approach and narrow nature of traditional comparative politics.

Utility of the Comparative Study of Politics

Video Part 1 · starts at 2:42:48

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • So at the end of the session you will be able to define the concept of comparative politics and we’ll be able to explain the utility of comparative politics.

Aristotle’s Comparative Tradition

  • So as we have discussed a lot about the meaning and definition of comparative politics.
  • Now we will focus on utility of comparative politics.
  • It means we will try to find out the reasons why comparative politics is very important for everyone.
  • So as a important discipline as a part of political science why do we study comparative politics and for that purpose we will find out the evolution of comparative politics.
  • Since times very ancient comparative politics has been a very popular and useful subject which study the broad political science with its concept with its behavior and various institutions as well.
  • So Aristotle observed the working of 158 constitutions and used the knowledge for answering such questions like which is an idol state which can be the best practicable state which is the best constitution.
  • Aristotle’s path was followed by his admirers and the tradition still continues.
  • So it can be stated that certainty that right from the days of Aristotle comparative study of political institutions, government processes has been developing as a major and popular area of investigation with a large number of political scientists.
  • After Aristotle several political thinkers like Cicero, Polybius, Machiavelli, Montesquieu, JS Mill etc. discussed and used the comparative methods in a highly productive way.

Systematic Development in the 20th Century & the New States

  • In the 20th century, the ancient tradition received a systematic use and development at the hands of larger number of political scientists and in the western world comparative politics came to be regarded as an autonomous discipline.
  • So several scholars of the third world countries which we call new states got engaged in the comparative politics studies and the exercise still continues.
  • So comparative politics continues to be a very popular area of the study and the increase in the number of sovereign independent states has made the task more interesting as well as challenging.
  • In fact, in contemporary times, the importance of comparative politics has increased tremendously.
  • So, the existence of 193 political systems and some non-state political entities focus more on the world which is has greatly enlarged the scope of political system and we talk about the comparative politics as well.
  • So the need to build a scientific theory of politics and the potential of comparative methods to serve this end has been mainly responsible for its increased importance.

The 19th Century & Historical Inquiry

  • So in the 19th century comparative politics were used by the political scientist for arriving at correct and valid conclusion regarding the nature and the organization of a state and government through the comparative study of organization powers and functions of various political institutions working in various states.
  • So the basic objective was to inquire into the historical and legal similarities and dissimilarities among the various forms of the government and their political institutions for getting answer of major questions like which form of government is best or was best which types of political institutions were the best.
  • So we can say that comparative study was regarded as the key to understanding of politics and consequently the key to provide answers of these questions.
  • A comparative normative perspective study of political institutions was conducted for answering these questions. the features, merits, demmerits, similarities and dissimilarities of political institutions which were compared and an attempt was made to identify the best political institution and this focus continued to remain popular up till the end of the 19th century.
  • But in the 20th century, the study of comparative politics underwent revolutionary changes.

From Traditional to Behavioural Focus

  • So the traditional focus means the comparative governments got replaced by a new direction.
  • Now the study of actual behavior of political institution and political processes came to be adopted as the main area of study and for this purpose several new concepts and approaches were developed and the building of a scientific theory of politics through comparative studies of politics came to the objective.
  • So through this you can understand the evolution of comparative politics with certain reasons.
  • Here you can also understand the difference between the traditional approach and the modern approach of comparative politics.
  • Now we will focus on the utility of comparative politics.
  • So the question of the usefulness of comparative politics is related to its usefulness and relevance to enhance understanding of political reality.
  • It aims to find out how comparative studies can help us better understand this reality.

Comparison in All Reasoning

  • So comparison forms the implicit part of all our reasonings and thinking.
  • But most comparison are conscious just to reach conclusion that comparative studies of politics can generalize.
  • So gathering information about a country is not enough just to make a generalization with some confidence.
  • So the stress in comparative politics lies in the theory building and testing with countries that act as a units or cases.
  • So emphasizing the accuracy, relevance and reliability of these signs, social scientists consider comparison to be the essential to social science because they provide a unique opportunity for control.

New Levels of Analysis in the Age of Globalisation

  • So in the study of social phenomena with the new debate about globalization new levels of analysis need to be considered and analysis of three or four levels cannot explain all effects and there are unlimited number of levels between the three major levels.
  • So individual levels of analysis identify the cause of an event in the direct circle of individual leaders or decision makers in a particular country.
  • It focuses on the human factors or human actors on the world stage that identify the characteristics of human decision making.
  • Domestic level analysis identifies the cause of the characteristics of the domestic system in a particular state.
  • Therefore, war is caused by aggressive or militant nations not by evil, incompetent or misguided people or we can say that the structure of power in the international system.
  • The another level is the systematic level of analysis which describe the result from the systemwide level including all states and we are seeking an explanation of international phenomena in consideration of the nature and the structure of the international political system during the survey period.
  • So global level factors are very similar to the system level factors but the main difference is that global factors are not always created by states whereas system factors are created by them only.
  • So global factors can be the result of individuals, stakeholders, groups, states, non-state actors and even natural condition but cannot be traced back to the actions of any state or group of these states.

Long History of Comparative Politics

  • So, comparative politics is the study and appraisal of domestic politics across countries.
  • Comparative politics has a long and very eminent history dating back just before the origin of systematic political studies in the ancient Greece and Rome.
  • Even ancient people compared their situations with those of other peoples and with whom they can came into contact.
  • So the ancient Greeks performed the earliest systematic comparison of a more modern and secular system.
  • Comparative politics which is a key area in political science pigeonholed by an empirical approach based on the comparative methods.
  • In our previous discussions we have discussed a lot about the meaning and definition of comparative politics.
  • To put it another way, we can say that comparative politics is the study of a domestic politics, political institutions and conflicts of the countries.

Comparisons Across & Within Countries

  • It often encompasses comparisons among countries and through tying within single countries emphasizing major patterns of similarity and differences.
  • Many political thinkers discussed majorly about the comparative politics.
  • It does not have functional focus in itself instead a methodological one.
  • So some thinkers believe that it is not having that kind of functionality.
  • In simple form comparative politics is not defined by the object of its study but by the method of it.

Politics Across Countries

  • So it applies to the study of political phenomena.
  • In the field of comparative politics, the term politics has three connotation such as political activities, political process and political power.
  • So political activity consists of the efforts by which the conditions of conflicts are created and resolved in a way pertaining to the interest of people as far as possible who play in their part in struggle for power. So we have discussed political activity. Now we’ll focus on political process.
  • Political process is an extension of political activity only. and political power is the major topic in comparative politics.

Power in Comparative Politics

  • So the term power has been defined by different writers in a different manner.
  • As we know that power is very important for everyone.
  • So some thinkers described power in a different manner like Pedrick described power as a certain kind of human relationship where Tuni explained power as a capacity of individual or groups of individual just to modify the conduct of other individuals in a manner which he desires. So it shows a kind of force or a control.

Comparative Study of Forms of Government

  • So when applied to particular fields of study, comparative politics denotes by other names such as comparative government or comparative politics.
  • So the comparative study of forms of government or we can say that comparative foreign policy comparing the foreign policies of different states in order to establish empirical connections between the characteristics of the state and the characteristics of its foreign policy.
  • Many theorists also articulated that comparative political science as a general term for an area of study as opposed to a methodology of study which can be seen as redundant.
  • So the political system only shows a political when either an overt or tactic comparison is being made.
  • So in this manner we can understand the nature and scope of comparative politics with its meaning also.
  • Now we will focus on levels of analyszis in comparative politics. So we will focus on the level of analysis. How do we compare?

Levels of Comparison

  • So the level of analysis in comparative politics include comparison of a single problem limited to the political systems that are homogeneous in character and operation.
  • Comparison of several elements or clusters of elements in relation to political system that are homogeneous.
  • Another thing is that comparison of institutions or segments of political process irrespective of homogeneity and at last comparison of a political systems.
  • So comparative analysis is a methodology within political science that is often used in the study of political systems, institutions and processes.
  • This can be done across a local, regional, national and international level.
  • Further, comparative analysis is grounded upon empirical evidence gathered from the recording and classification of real life political phenomena.
  • Comparative research aims to develop greater political understanding through a scientifically constrained methodology often referred to as one of the three largest of fields of political science and it is a field of study that was referred to as the greatest intellectual achievement by Edward A. Freeman.

Single-Country & Group Comparisons

  • Comparative analysis can be employed on either a single country or a case or group of countries.
  • For the study of one country to be considered comparative, it is essential that the findings of the research are referred into a larger framework which engage in a systematic comparison of analogous phenomena.
  • So there are some patterns, similarities and differences which are examined to assess the relationship of variance between the two or more separate systems.
  • So it is the nature of analysis that renders it comparative.

Interdisciplinary & Empirical Research

Use of Other Social Sciences

  • It studies politics with the help of other social sciences like psychology, sociology, anthropology and economics.

The Comparative Approach to Social Research

  • So the concept of comparative method here we can discuss the comparative approach to social research has a long tradition dating back to the ancient Greece which we have discussed in our previous sessions as well.
  • Since the 19th century, philosophers, anthropologist, political scientist and sociologist have used crosscultural comparison to achieve different objectives in understanding social processes.
  • So the phrase comparison method refers to a method of comparing different societies or groups within the same society to indicate if they are different in certain respect and why they are different from each other.
  • So the comparison method had a long been regarded as the predominant method of sociology and therefore the so-called comparison method is the process of comparing different things in similar but known ways.
  • A comparative approach in sociological research is one of the research methods for studying the formation and transformation of the society.

Contrasting Social Structures & Data Collection

  • So comparative studies examine the contrast social structures and processes across countries and reasons to identify common patterns.
  • In the comparison method, researchers collect data about different social groups and compare one group to another just to identify what is apparent in one group but not in another group.
  • So the principles behind such methods come from positivist who seeks to isolate and identify the causes of social events and behavior.
  • Dai was the one who discussed about the study of society was an example of comparative method by comparing official statistic between different societies.
  • He argued that he was able to identify what was apparent in one society rather than another which could cause suicide.
  • So for researchers who adopt a normative perspective, comparison have served as a tool for developing classification of social phenomena and establishing whether shared phenomena can be explained by the same cause.
  • So a constant comparison method is labeled attached to a procedures designed to analyze qualitative data and this process is one of the processes that continuously compare segments of data with each other.
  • So such processes lead to the emergence of categories and help clarify the relationship between the them and so that models of social processes can be developed.

Aristotle’s Adoption of the Comparative Method

Video Part 1 · starts at 3:08:32

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • At the end of the session, you will be able to define the concept of comparative politics and will be able to explain Aristotle’s adoption of comparative methods.

Recap: Comparative Politics

  • So as in our previous discussions, we have discussed a lot about comparative politics, its meaning, definition, nature and scope.
  • So we can easily understand what is comparative politics all about.
  • So comparative politics is related to the comparison.
  • It’s a conscious comparison of various political structures, political systems, various institutions with certain behaviors as well.
  • So we know very well that comparative politics started with a vast scope of analyzing various political structures, various institutions, various approaches related to the political system.
  • Comparative politics is as old as political science.

Aristotle as Father of Political Science

  • Aristotle, the father of political science, used comparative methods for comprehending and analyzing principles, issues and problems of the Greek city states.
  • So he used the knowledge gained for building his theory of politics.
  • By following Aristotle, several political thinkers begin using the comparative methods for analyzing and presenting their views and conclusions about politics.
  • Thus, it can be legitimately observed that comparative politics had its origin with Aristotle only.
  • Harry Eckstein has rightly observed comparative politics has a particular right just to claim Aristotle as an ancestor because of the primacy he assigned to politics among these sciences and because the problems he raised and the methods he used are similar to those still current in political studies.
  • But in contemporary times, comparative politics stand recognized as a primary and essential dimension of the study of politics.
  • A large number of political scientists even regard it as autonomous discipline because of its vast scope and importance for comprehensive understanding which is related to politics in all societies.
  • So in this manner we can say that the comparative approach has emerged as very useful and highly popular approach for the study of politics.
  • So comparative study of political system forms an integral part of the study of politics. Aristotle has a great love for facts.

Aristotle’s Empirical Method

  • Due to biological training of his childhood and youth, his scientific studies, he grew into a careful and systematic observer.
  • The study of politics becames empirical in the hands of Aristotle.
  • So it is interesting to note that the very first para of politics begins with words observation shows us first and this clearly indicates the use of empirical methods in his writings.
  • So this method is also known as inductive method and it includes the study of scientific events on the basis of these specific observation and generalized principle is formed by this only.
  • Aristotle also used the comparative method in the study of political phenomena.
  • He had extraordinary knowledge of political institution both of his own times and of the past.
  • So it is said that he had studied about 200 constitutions almost. Aristotle used scientific methods of politics.
  • He combines induction from facts with deductive reasoning from first principle.
  • Beside insisting on the necessity of a systematic collection of facts as the first requisite of the scientific methods in the study of political phenomena.
  • So Aristotle also laid emphasis on the importance of measuring their significance and value.

Aristotle’s Empirical Method: Politics

  • The study of politics is empirical by the hands of Aristotle as we have explained previously.
  • So it is interesting to note that the first paragraph of politics begins with the observation shows us first and he can also explain it in a different manner where he was focusing more on observation where he was focusing more on observation and empirical methods.
  • This clearly shows that empirical methods are used in his writing and this method is also known as induction.
  • Another aspect of Aristotle method is noteworthy and that is respect for his tradition.

Conservative & Reformist Elements in Aristotle

  • What makes him conservative and prevents him from the becoming plateau is his loyalty to the facts of the past history. And he’s more reformist in his opinion.
  • A person who value the accumulated wisdom of the past cannot be a revolutionary.
  • The overall bend and prejudice of his mind has led Aristotle just to believe that in order to be effective the idol must not get against the real situation.
  • So here he was very much practical with idealistic also.
  • So there was a combination of two important features or characteristics.
  • So he included in politics with real politics and the idealistic politics.
  • Therefore the contradiction and conflicts between theory and fact were much more difficult for Aristotle than for Plateau.
  • Aristotle emphasizes the importance of measuring their importance or value and he argued that in addition to studying the forms we envisioned in the past and we must investigate the purpose of the state.
  • In other words, the true method of politics must be teleological as well as historical.

David Apter’s View on Comparative Politics

Video Part 1 · starts at 3:15:55

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • At the end of the session you will be able to define the concept of comparative politics and will be able to explain David Apter’s view on comparative politics.

David Apter: Democratic & Totalitarian Views

  • So here we will start with the view related to the comparative politics which discussed by David Apter.
  • David Apter drew a line of difference between democratic and totalitarian varieties showing a functional interpretation of the political system.
  • According to him, in a democratic regime, the government acts as a means of relieving tensions and seeks to resolve the crisis.
  • So he focused on majorly two areas for relieving tensions and seeks to resolve the crisis.
  • On the contrary, the totalitarian model, the government tries to deal with institutionalization, socialization, and internalization by mobilizing people.

Political Modernisation as Cultural & Institutional Change

  • Political modernization can be described as changes in political culture and institution that result from the process of modernization.
  • And therefore it also means a change in political culture in response to changes in the social and physical environment.
  • So where we are focusing on modernization or changes we are talking about social and physical environment.
  • Political modernization is described as a systematic, sustainable and powerful application of human energy to the rational control of human physical and social environments for a variety of human purpose.
  • Modern society is a society which is based on the spirit of advanced technology and science and is a rational view of life. a secular approach to social relations, a sense of justice in public affairs and where the nation state is a prime unit of politics.
  • There are many aspects to the concept of modernization and therefore political modernization also has many aspect.

Political Modernisation: Psychological & Intellectual Change

  • Number one, at the psychological level, there are changes in the people’s attitude and orientation.
  • At the intellectual level there is the expansion and diffusion of knowledge.
  • Demographically there are changes in the people’s mobility and at the same time at social level individual loyalties shift from primary to secondary.
  • Many times at economic level it means not only industrialization but also improvement in agriculture, trade and commerce.
  • So while talking about change David Apter focus on the psychological, intellectual, demographic, social and economic level to understand the change within the society and within the political system.
  • Political modernization is seen as the result of social mobilization and economic development.
  • Therefore the term political modernization can be explained as positive term in which changes occurs to improve society.
  • It also refers to the state of political change that European countries are experiencing today followed by many countries in different parts of the world.
  • Now we will discuss the stages of political modernization.
  • So here you can see the stages of political modernization which are explained by David and discuss certain stages of modernization and there are few stages.

Contact & Control Stage

  • Number one is the contact and control stage and this stage is shaped by a small number of individuals guided by a desire for mission or adventure.
  • Here we may mention adventures taken by colonist in England, France, Germany and other countries.
  • But thanks to their adventures, new technologies have spread and brought about trade and commercial growth.
  • So you can see the impact of contact and control with the help of some certain changes within the society. reaction and a reaction stage.

Second Stage: Colonial Influence

  • In the second stage of political modernization, we can say that we saw a kind of colonialist come into closer contact with indigenous peoples and closer contact with indigenous people help them to implement their policies.
  • So, colonies also benefit from the rule of colonial power.
  • But at the same point the natives find the way they are being exploited by colonial rulers and they try to keep in touch with the colonial rulers and establish various organization just to raise their demands.
  • So the result is a national leader from indigenous people.
  • Another stage is contradiction and liberation stage.
  • So we can call it a third stage of modernization which made or makes the conflict between indigenous people and colonial rulers.
  • The national movement spread and because of that movement the demand for sharing of administrative powers rised and colonial forces now devised new administrative policies.

Nationalist Leaders & Independence

  • But nationalist leaders are pursuing their demand for more independence. and at last search for solution.
  • So this stage occurs after the arrival of independence.
  • In this phase a second revolution for social economic changes takes place and however the political system faces new challenges.
  • The leaders of the nationalist struggle also lose their charisma.

Liberalism as the Dominant Ideology

  • So in modern times liberalism is the most dominant ideology in the world.
  • It represent peace, friendship or fraternity, freedom, cooperation and justice available to people all over the world.
  • And therefore liberalism can be equated with internationalism which represent the peaceful resolution of international conflicts and abandonment of war as a means of national policy nonaggression and the non-intervention of internal affairs of one nation.
  • So here we are focusing more on peace, security, cooperation and these are the important features or the part of the liberal view where it focus more on fraternity, cooperation and justice.
  • The liberal ideology also represents the value of freedom, democracy and humanism.

Three Basic Assumptions: Limited Government

  • And there are three basic assumptions. Number one, it talks about limited government. Number two, pluralistic society.
  • And the third one is unlimited scope for human actions.
  • So as we know very well that when we talk about liberal theory, it focus more on the limited type of government where government can only provide freedom and it can also secure or protect the freedom of the people.
  • So government is having a very limited role to maintain law and order to have a social rules and regulation for maintaining proper order within the society for a better system and for protecting the liberty and freedom of the people. Number two, we talk about pluralistic society. Yes, of course.
  • When we talk about liberal theory, they believe that within the pluralistic society there are certain groups, there are various groups and they do have so many issues related to that and that’s why we have a state which has responsibility to resolve all the conflict within the pluralistic society with various opinions with various participation they live together. unlimited scope for human actions.
  • As we know that we are talking about freedom, we are talking about cooperation, we are focusing on fraternity, it means we are giving huge vast scope for human actions where they can live freely and they can talk about their holistic development where we are focusing on the mental or the physical development of human being freely.

Assumptions of Liberal Theory

  • Right?
  • So as we have discussed these three assumptions related to liberal theory, now we can discuss the liberal ideology which becomes the dominant ideology in the western world.
  • Advances in the social, political and economic systems since the 17th century which have contributed to the growth of liberal ideologies.
  • The ideology of liberalism has gone through various stages and has evolved since the 20th century as a doctrine of liberal democracy, democratic capitalism and modern liberalism.
  • But a common factor in them is that they all advocate policies and actions designed to protect and promote the values of rights, freedoms and personalities.
  • So liberalism reduced state control over individual as much as possible and supported as the key to progress the three basic principles of free and happy society and these are free competition, free trade and freedom of choice.
  • With this only we can have a freedom within the our life where state can only work as a regulating body for maintaining law and order for maintaining system for proper allocation of resources and services with complete freedom and for the protection of the freedom state is working as a important entity within the society.

The Marxian Approach

  • So as in our previous discussion we discussed the liberal methodology and you can here understand the difference between liberal and Marxist methodology with the detailed study of Marxist methodology in the comparative politics.
  • The Marxian approach is another important modern approach of political science and research that is also applied in the field of comparative politics.
  • A surprising feature of this approach is that the central theme of political science here is the state and which is considered as an inevitable result of class contradiction.
  • So this approach believes in the fact that the stateless state of social life is born as the final stage of social development and this approach assumes that economic dominates the scenes of political scenarios.
  • In our previous discussion we discussed the liberal methodology where we focused more on freedom, fraternity and cooperation with justice.
  • But here Marxist is having a very different perspective for understanding the nature of state for understanding the functions of state and for discussing the role of the state as well.
  • So while talking about state and its importance Marx was in favor of a stateless and classless society as Marx considers state as inevitable result of class contradiction or class conflict.
  • So this approach assumes that economic scene or economic position is having a control over all these substructures like political, social, religious and other structures.
  • So basically Marx; according to Marx, economy is the base on which there are so many superructures are designed or created.
  • Therefore, as the economic system determines the class structure and the means of production, distribution and exchange, the relationship between the master and the slave and the feudal lord in the ser will change accordingly.

Historical Materialism

  • This is what he discussed with the historical evolution or his historical materialism while talking about the changes within the society. But the nature of the society was not changing. Initially it was related to the king and slave.
  • Later on with the feudals and surf and after this with the modernization we can see or witness industrialization with the help of this capitalist class and the labor class.

Competition Between Classes

  • Therefore this approach primarily focuses on the competition between two competing classes. the ones that have and the ones that don’t have. It means halves and halves not.
  • This state of contradiction can only be ended by the establishment of socialist social order.
  • And for that purpose, Marx was in favor of revolution, a kind of revolution or rebellion approach to attack the capitalist section or capitalist group for maintaining that kind of equality within the system.
  • Therefore from the above discussion we have already understood the fact that this particular approach not only focuses on the facts of social contradiction but also find solution to them also.
  • According to the Marxist approach the state is basically a class organization.
  • Therefore the ruling class controls political power and the state is the embodiment of that power.
  • And that’s why this ruling class or the political category use state as a tool for suppressing and for the exploitation of those classes.
  • Therefore, the nation is subordinate to the economic power and the nation is a tool devised by the ruling class for its own benefits and it is the appropriate to mention here that Karl Marx was the main index of Marxian approach.

Behaviorism & Political Science

  • So behaviorism is seen as modern approach just to study the political science.
  • However, this approach emerged in the 20th century.
  • An important consideration of activism was the study of political behavior as a field of study in political science.
  • So it focuses on individuals as voters, leaders, revolutionaries, party members and the influence of group or political system on the political behavior of individuals.
  • Behaviorism firmly emphasized the scientific objective and value-free study of environmentally conditioned political events and the behavior of individuals involved in the phenomena.
  • Therefore, it focuses on the role of individual behavior and the scientific analysis at various levels.
  • So behaviorism is the different of methods related to the development of methods for traditional politics that did not take into account human behavior as an actor in politics.

Behavioralism vs Behaviorism

  • So behavioralism is a quite different from behaviorism.
  • It is narrow in its application and refers to the reaction of an organism caused by some stimulus.
  • It does not consider the role of emotions, thoughts and prejudices that determine the individual’s reaction.
  • Behaviorism takes into account the roles of emotions, ideas and prejudices.

Behavioural Approach: Davis’s Distinction

  • So here we are talking about the behavioral approach where we are focusing more on the behavioral aspect of a human being while studying the political system.
  • David distinguish between behavioralism or behaviorism using an example.
  • According to him, there are some adopted behavioral scholars for example stimulus response or the organism response.
  • So David believes that the behavioral revolution is the intellectual tendency of political scientist to empirically study a person’s political behavior as well.
  • So now here we will discuss some features related to behavioralism.
  • David Easton described certain important features of behaviorism and that are considered its intellectual foundation and they are regularities.
  • This approach believes that there is a someone or some coherence in the political behavior that can be expressed in generalization or theory in order to elucidate and predict political phenomena.
  • Verification behaviorist do not want to accept everything that has been established.
  • Therefore, they emphasize testing and verifying everything.
  • So according to them if the phenomena is not verified it is not scientific.

Research Techniques

  • They also focus on techniques.
  • So behavioral scientists focus on the use of these research tools and methods just to generate valid tools and reliable comparative data.
  • Quantification after collecting data researchers need to measure and quantify those data as well. values. They focus more on values.
  • Like behavioral scholars have emphasized the separation of facts from values and they believe that in order to do objective research, it must be value-free.
  • That means researcher should not have prejudice or prejudices.

Systematisation in Behavioral Research

  • Systematization.
  • According to behavioral scientist, political science research must be systematic.
  • Pure science which is the another characteristic of behaviorism was its purpose to make political science a pure science.
  • It means we believe that political science research should be verified by evidence.
  • So these are the basic features which we have discussed for related to behavioralism.
  • Now we will focus on behavioral scientists which have made significant achievements in the development and improvement of research tools and techniques and these developments were made in the fields of content analysis, case analysis, interviewing and observation and statistic.

Achievements of Behavioralism in Comparative Politics

Video Part 1 · starts at 3:40:21

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • So at the end of the session you will be able to understand the achievements of behavioral movement in comparative politics.

Behavioralism as a Reaction to the Traditional Approach

  • So in this session we will discuss the achievements of behavioralism in comparative politics.
  • behavioralism came into existence as a reaction against the traditional approaches of political inquiry and partly as a result of the quest in search for more scientific methods for acquiring empirical knowledge during the political analysis.
  • behavioralism could be interpreted as a renewed and reinforced effort just to take the science in political science seriously on the lines of physical and biological sciences and in line with new developments in the field of psychology and other social sciences.
  • Behavioralism or behavioral approach to the analysis and explanation of political phenomena is particularly associated with the work of American political scientist after the second world war but its origin can be traced back to the works of Brahim and Arthur.
  • Behavioralism became popular with the social scientists in the postwar period also with both negative and positive reasons.
  • Negatively behavioralism set itself against mere description like raw empiricis like raw empiricism.

Against Metaphysics & Abstract Speculation

  • Simple factualism against metaphysics. abstract speculation and deduction from first principles.
  • So against the grand interpretation of history but it has some positive reasons also.
  • So positively it favored studying successful science just to learn how to apply proper scientific modes of thought and methods to the research focusing attention on actual observable behavior.
  • Thus, behavioralism aims to produce a body of knowledge that aspire to provide verified principles of human behavior through the use of methods similar to those natural sciences.
  • So this methodological identity and essential unity of human phenomena could lead to the integration of social sciences and the development of unified science of society.
  • In this manner, the aim of at least some leading exponent of social science is to build up a body of scientific knowledge which could be used for controlling the social environment for the scientific management of society.
  • So in this manner we can say that the result of behaviorism can be traced into two areas.
  • One is research technology and second is research methodology and theory building.

Rigorous Comparative Case Analysis

  • So behavioral scholars have also made remarkable achievements in the field of rigorous comparative case analysis.
  • Bruce Risset for example have developed a rigorous comparative case analysis framework for the study of deterrence.
  • He had the opinion that it could be applied in favor of many other situations as well.
  • Gladden and William did a remarkable job in this area by applying this method to the Supreme Court’s game and doing a comparative analysis of each party and their party system.
  • So the study of comparative politics has been significantly facilitated through cross country research.
  • Also behavioralist contribution to theory building are not commendable because they are primarily related to individuals and group behavior but also they are paying less and less attention to the state governments and agencies.
  • So the main purpose of behavioral scholar is not only to find out the systematic theories.
  • Only a small proportion of behavioral studies of political science conducted by Max Weber and Mannheim are sociologist or anthropologist who are interested more in the study of individual rather than the state of society because they are devoted to history and philosophy.

Criticism of Behavioralism in Comparative Politics

Video Part 1 · starts at 3:45:30

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • At the end of the session, you will be able to understand the criticism of behavioralism in comparative politics.

What Is Behavioralism?

  • So before discussing the criticism of behavioralism, first we will try to understand what is behavioralism.
  • So behavioralism is an approach in political science that emerged in 1930s in the United States.
  • It represented a sharp break from the previous approaches in emphasizing an objective quantified approach to explain and predict the political behavior.
  • So we can say that it is associated with the rise of behavioral science modeled after the natural sciences.
  • Behavioralism claims it can explain political behavior from an unbiased neutral point of view.
  • So behavioralist seeks to examine the behavior acts or actions of the individuals rather than the characteristics of the institutions such as legislature, executive and judiciaries and other groups in different social settings and to explain in this behavior as it relates to the political system.
  • So overall we can say that when we talk about behavioralism it is only related to the individual actions their behavior or their acts which is related to the political system.

Behavioralism in Comparative Politics

  • Now we’ll talk about the behavioralism in comparative politics.
  • So empirical study of politics based on reality which has been termed as behavioral approach to politics.
  • It entails studying the actual behavior of an individuals and groups.
  • As we have discussed previously, playing political roles instead of merely examining the laws, rules and ideological patterns.
  • We can say that behavioralism has been one of the most important development in the political science during the 20th century.
  • So the study of political behavior in USA started with Graham Wallas and AF Bentley who advocated the study of actual phenomena of politics in 198.
  • Graham Wallas held the politics without the study of psychology of individual was meaningless.
  • So he said clearly that without knowing the human psychology their actions and behavior we can’t understand the political system.

Role of Groups: Bentley

  • So it is useless as a behavior played an important role in a political phenomena.
  • Bentley highlighted its significant role among groups.
  • Also according to semil behavioralism may be defined as the systematic search for political patterns through the formulation of empirical theory and technical analysis with verification.
  • According to Hines, modern behavioral science is eminently concerned not only the acts of the men but also his cognitive effective and evaluative process.
  • So behavior in political fields refer to political actions not simply or directly observable political actions but also the perceptual motivational and attitudinal components of the behavior which makes men’s political identification demands and his system of political benefits values and goals.
  • David Easton an important exponent of behavioralist school of political science has highlighted eight important features of behavioralism in which we can discuss few.
  • Number one is the regularities in human behavior.
  • So we have to find out the regularity in human behavior.
  • Number two a preference for verification and testing rather than taking things for granted.
  • Application of correct techniques for acquisition and interpretation of scientific data which emphasize on measurement and quantification for predicting a political result.

Belief in Value-Free Study

  • Belief in value-free study.
  • Belief in a systematic study of political science which should be theory oriented and theory directed. also insistence on pure science approach and belief that social and political phenomena cannot be studied in isolation and therefore an interdisciplinary approach is very important.
  • Even after finding so many positive points about behavioralism, we can see so much criticism about the behavioralism in comparative politics.
  • So behavioralists have been criticized mainly on the following grounds.
  • Behavioralism is concerned more with techniques rather than results.
  • Number two, behavioralism is directed at pseudo politics as it advocates personal or private interest at the cost of universal interest or common interest.

Neglect of Institutions

  • Behavioralists have neglected the effects of institutions on society and targeted their efforts mostly on behavioral aspect only of the individuals and group. So it is not focusing more on the institution.
  • It is only focusing on the behavior of the individual and groups.
  • Politics can never be value-free as claimed by the behavioralist.
  • behavioralists have been focusing mainly on static subjects rather than the current problems.
  • So they have ignored urgent and important problems because these does not suit their study.

Mechanical View of Human Motivation

  • There are few difficulties associated with the ever-changing behavior of a men and no correct prediction can be made about future behavior of individuals and groups.
  • Behavioralist approach to political science depends so much on other branches of social science like sociology and anthropology and we can say that psychology that every identity integrity and autonomy of political science is threatened.
  • The behavioralist place too much emphasis on political behavior of men but do not apply their research to their current issues and problems.
  • Behavioralism provides only a limited knowledge about the political behavior of a man but does not provide real knowledge just to solve the urgent issues facing by the world. So it is not talking about the current issues.
  • It is only talking about the human behavior and their actions.
  • The traditionalist among the political scientists have been leveled the following criticism about behavioralism and some of which may overlap with those noted point points.

Ignoring Larger World Issues

  • Behavioralists assume a mechanical view of men motivated by self-interest alone and they ignore human values and norms.
  • Not only this, they ignore bigger issues of the world or the current issues of the world.
  • They ignore theoretical aspects of the subject and it is only concerned about the techniques.
  • Human behavior cannot be generalized as assumed by the behavioralist.
  • Behavioralists give more attention to statistical figures than the human ideals.
  • Study of politics can never be value-free and it is very clear that you need to involve values in political system.
  • The analysis of behavioralist is defective because they consider American institution as the best of the world and use behavioralism as a tool to prove the worth of those institutions.

Traditional vs Modern Approaches to Comparative Politics

Video Part 1 · starts at 3:53:53

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • At the end of this session, you will be able to define the differences between traditional and modern approaches just of to comparative politics.

Comparative Politics & Its Methodologies

  • Comparative politics as we have discussed a lot about comparative politics.
  • So you can understand that political system which actually compare the different political system within the society and within other countries.
  • So comparative politics focuses on understanding and explaining domestic politics from a comparative perspective.
  • So it is one of the three main sub fields of political science along with political theory and international affairs.
  • Political science uses many broad theoretical approaches and methodologies.
  • Comparative politics provide a systematic, coherent and practical way to understand and better understand what is happening in the world around us.
  • So you can compare their political system, you can understand their role of political system within their societies and through this you can understand in a better manner what is happening around the world.

Relevance of Comparative Politics

  • So moreover in a broad sense comparative politics is relevant to almost everyone even those who are not interested in foreign studies.
  • So the comparative politics approach can be applied to a wide variety of problems in a variety of disciplines from mundane to subline.
  • Comparative politics is not surprising as a method of study. It presupposes comparison only.
  • As a subject of research, comparative politics focuses on and explaining political phenomena that occur within a state, society, country or political system.
  • So defining comparative politics as a method of comparative system and defining the subject of research based on the investigation the national political phenomena highlights several important points.
  • First this area is primarily concerned with the internal or domestic dynamics and helps to distinguish between comparative politics and international affairs.
  • Second, we talk about comparative politics which shows that it is properly related to political phenomena only.
  • And third, and perhaps most importantly, it shows that the field is not only characterized but defined by comparative analysis methods.

The Traditional Approach

  • There is a traditional approach to political science which is broadly predominant till the occurrence of the second world war.
  • These approaches were mainly associated with the traditional outlook of politics which underlined the study of the state and government.
  • Consequently, the traditional approaches are principally concerned with the study of organization and activities of the state and principles and the ideas which motivate political organization and their activities.
  • So these approaches were normative and principled enough.
  • According to them, the study of political science should be limited to the formal structures of the government like laws, rules and regulations.
  • And therefore the supporters of the traditional approaches stress various norms such as what ought to be or should be rather than what is all about.
  • So while traditional approaches are mostly of legal, institutional, historical and descriptive nature, modern approaches are more scientific, rational and analytical.

Institutional Comparison & Its Limits

  • They compare the functions performed by different institutions and these approaches lack social, economic, cultural and behavioral factors.
  • The traditional approach is value based and focuses on including value in the study of political phenomena.
  • So there are proponents of this approach who believe that political science research should be based solely on facts as facts and values are linked to each other.
  • And since the days of Plateau and Aristotle, the major political issues has revolved around the normative direction.

Characteristics of Traditional Approaches

  • And therefore there are many traditional approaches which have developed such as legal approach, philosophical approach, historical approach, institutional approach etc.
  • Now we will find out the characteristics of traditional approaches.
  • Traditional approaches are mostly normative and stresses on the values of the politics.
  • In the traditional approach, prominence is on the study of different formal political structures only. within the traditional approaches which are made very little attempt to relate theory and research and these approaches consider that since facts and values are closely interlinked studies in political science can never be scientific.
  • Now we will see the difference between traditional and modern approaches to comparative politics.
  • So there is a difference on the basis of their definition.
  • When we talk about traditional approach, traditional thinkers consider political science to be a study of state and government only.
  • As Garner wrote, political science begin with the state and ends with the state only.
  • In contrast, modern thinkers view politics as a study of human political behavior.
  • As lost will said that politics is a study of the formation and sharing of power on the basis of their nature they are different.
  • Traditional thinkers of political science consider it as art and focus on what it should be.
  • As Buckle believed in the current state of knowledge of political science far from science it is the most lacking art.
  • From ancient times, Indian political thinkers like Manu, Chanakya, Shukra have defined it as the art of governance while ancient Greek thinkers like Plateau also saw it as a government art.
  • On the other hand, modern political thinkers see politics as a science and focus on the objective and mathematical studies of relevant database.

Differences in Research Methods

  • You can also see the difference on the basis of their research and methods.
  • Traditional political science uses philosophical, historical and comparative methods for the research and these methods are old and unsophisticated.
  • On the other hand, modern political science research focus on the interdisciplinary methods based on statistical experiences.
  • So modern political thinkers analyze and predict human political behavior and associated with truths and its experiences.

Values in Traditional vs Modern Approaches

  • When you see the traditional and modern approaches different so on the basis of the values you can see a difference in them.
  • The traditional view of political science has no values.
  • It has influenced by the subjective and personal approach.
  • So traditional thinkers believed in the ethics and morality.
  • The modern view of political science does not emphasize on even values.
  • In modern view, political thinkers move away from morals, values, ethics and prejudices and continue to study using scientific methods for analyzing the political system.

Differences in Purpose & Aim

  • On the basis of purpose and aim, they are different.
  • The purpose of traditional political science is to achieve a good life.
  • The purpose of modern political science on the other hand is not to live a good life but to explain political events from their true perspective.
  • So the modern political thinkers believe that political system cannot be passive spectators but they must try to solve all the political and other issues within the society.
  • There is a difference on the basis of scope also.
  • Under traditional political system, the present, past and future of a nation are studied along with government agencies, methods and responsibilities.
  • On the other hand, modern political thinkers do not study institutions but methods also.
  • They like to study who enacts the laws and the actual process of the law rather than studying the administration and parliament.
  • So on the basis of this we can say that we have discussed and learned the differences between traditional and modern approaches to comparative politics.

The Philosophical Approach as a Conventional Method

  • Philosophical approach which is conventional approach to study of politics.
  • Mostly the study of politics was subjugated by philosophical reflections on several and universal political values that were regarded as essential to the just state and the good state only.
  • So the oldest approach to the study of politics is the philosophical approach.
  • Philosophy is the study or a science of truths or principles underlying all the knowledge and being.
  • So it entails that philosophy or philosophical approach tries to explore the truth of political incidents or events.
  • So in this manner we can say that the main aim of political philosophical approach is to evaluate the consequences of the events in a logical and scientific manner.
  • The philosophical approach is another traditional or classical approach for studying politics.
  • Philosophy has many definition one of which is the study or a science of the truth or principles underlying all knowledge and existence.
  • It means that philosophical approach seeks to find the truth of a political events.

Purpose of the Philosophical Approach

  • It explore the purpose of political writing or the purpose of political writers.
  • So the purpose of philosophical approach is to analyze the outcome of the case in a logical and scientific way.
  • According to Wende political philosophy means thinking in a way broader sense it presents a general concept of a purpose and means purpose and methods.
  • So we can say that philosophy is all about thinking or a thought process to understand and to find out the purpose means and their methods.
  • The purpose of the philosophical approach is to be a political philosopher.

Clarifying Terms Through Philosophy

  • Clarifying the words and terms used by research initiated by philosophical approach removes confusion about assumptions.
  • So an important plus point of philosophical approach is that it goes deep into every aspect of political phenomena and analyze them evenly.
  • So interpretation of political activity arouses interest in the minds of political students and scholars.
  • So the word and phrases used by philosophers shed light on the subject and the content.
  • This approach seeks to understand our fundamental nature and aims as human beings recognizing principles and standards of right conduct in political life.
  • So it is a normative in character and believes in developing norms or certain standards about the system.

Logical Method & Language

  • It followed the logical method where investigators has his own values and determined philosophies.
  • So in this manner the philosophical approach is claimed to increase linguistic clarity and therefore this approach is said to be aimed at thinking or a thought system.
  • So the method applied by philosophical approach is logical analysis. It uses reason to find the truth and the truth.
  • The truth that this approach establishes or discovers can be various types such as normative and descriptive.
  • In modern Greek city states of plateaus, morals, values and idealism have fallen to the point where he was so shocked that he seriously considered reviving them.
  • He wanted to establish the politics and morality were not the opposite concepts.
  • Rather an idol and moral political unity can be made a reality through selfless control by the philosophical king.
  • John Locke wrote his second treaty of justice the interest and purpose of the new middle class and he struggled for freedom. was also Machiavelli and Hobbes wrote to support the absolutism of the royal family.

Reason & the Search for Truth

  • So in this manner we can say that philosophical approach use procedures of logical analysis.
  • It uses reason to explore the truth and the truth which this approach established may be various kinds like normative, descriptive and prescriptive.
  • But the philosophical approach is indifferent to the nature of category of truth.
  • And this approach also tries to establish standards of good, right and just based on their thinking and thought process.
  • So many critics observe that this approach determines what is in the interest of the public and he identifies interest more with the ends that with the means.
  • So it is well established that philosophical approach helps people to understand the contemporary history and the nature of politics suggested by philosophers.
  • In other words, the philosophical approach aids to comprehend the political ideology of the past centuries.
  • In this manner, the philosophical approach is very important for researchers and people.
  • Application of the philosophical approach in political science focuses on the great ideas, values and doctrines of politics.
  • So the normative philosophical approach is the ancient and least scientific approach to the study of politics and it has been taken over although not completely displayed not completely displaced by contemporary theory.

The Historical Approach: Sabine’s Perspective

Linking the Past with the Present

  • So in this manner every past is linked with the present and thus the historical analysis provides a chronological order of every political phenomena.
  • The term historical approach to politics may be used in two major senses.
  • Firstly, it may denote the process of arriving at the laws governing politics through an analysis of historical events that is events of the past as exemplified by the theories propounded by Hegel and Marx.
  • In the second place, historical approach stands for an attempt of understanding politics through a historical account of political thought of the past as exemplified by the George H. Sabine.
  • So there is a approach which talks about the history, historical events and history system.
  • So there is an approach which talks about historical events and facts.

History as a Record of the Past

  • History as a document consists of documentaries and other major proofs that have taken place in the past.
  • With regard to the historical approach, we focus our attention on the historical events reported in the documentation.
  • So a characteristics of historical approach is that history as a written or recorded subject or a document focuses on the past events.
  • From history, we come to know what humans were like in the past and what he is now. So history is a treasure trove of events.

Understanding Political Theory Through Historical Factors

  • This approach states that political theory can be only understood when the historical factors are taken into consideration where we can focus on the values and effects of the history or past.
  • It highlights on the study of history of every political reality to analyze any situation.
  • So political theorists like Maitland, Sabine and Jennings believe that politics and history are interlinked with each other and therefore the study of politics always should have historical viewpoint with itself.
  • Sabine considered the political science should include all those subject which have been discussed in the writings of different political thinkers since the beginning.
  • So history defines about the past as well as links it with the present events also.
  • So without studying the past political events, institutions and the political environment, conditions and situation, the analysis of the present would remain largely imperfect.
  • So main attribute to historical approach is the history as a written or recorded subject and which focus on the past events.
  • History is the storehouse of the events from the profiles, autobiographies, descriptions by authors and journalist.
  • Investigators know what event occurred in the past.

Politically Revealing Events as Sources

  • So it is to be prominent that the event must have political revealing or they must be politically significant also.
  • So these events provide the best materials upon which theory and principles of political science are built.
  • History communicates research, how government, political parties and many others institutions worked, their success and failure and from these they receive lessons which guide them in determining the future course of action.
  • So we can say that history is not just a record of past events or achievements but interpretation, comments and explanations made by historians also.

Appropriate Sources for Political Scientists

Tradition & Practices of the Political Community

Accumulation of Evidence

  • One is the very nuing of facts and other is the interpretation of the facts and phenomena.

The Historian’s Handling of Facts

Gaps in Political History

Documentation vs Interpretation of Facts
