  • So the acceleration of the evidences is to be judged from a proper perspective.
  • The implication is that adequate care should be taken while evaluating evidence and facts and such a caution is not always strictly followed by so many people.
  • So the historical facts do not serve the purpose of those who use it and this is the main complaint or the critical area against the historical approach to the study of politics.

History as a Record of Great Men

Institutional Approach – Historical Overview

Institutional Approach & Its Link with the Legal Approach

  • Here in this presentation we will discuss the institutional approach historical overview.
  • Institutional approach which is closely related to legal approach yet it is very different.
  • Significantly this approach does not solely bank on other discipline like philosophy, history or law for understanding politics.
  • Amongst the traditional approaches, it alone gives independent identity to the systematic study of politics.
  • Amongst the traditional approaches, it alone gives independent identity to the systematic study of politics.
  • Institutional approach lays stress on the study of political institution and structures like executive, legislature, judiciary, political parties, interest group etc.
  • Among the ancient thinkers, Aristotle is an important contributor to this approach while the modern thinkers include James Bryce, Bentley, Walter Bagehot, Harold Laski, Harold Laski etc.

An Everyday Approach to Political Research

  • An institutional approach to political research is very common today and according to Westby it is important.
  • So readers, scholars, researchers or even the general public are accustomed to looking at politics from an institutional perspective.
  • The institutional approach is called the structural approach and according to MacIver the institution is an established form of procedure.
  • So institutions associate structures with the machine to organize, direct and carry out the wide variety of activities that human society needs to meet human needs.
  • By this definition, families, governments, states and all kinds of organization that thrive within the state are institutions and therefore the system is created to meet human requirements. political parties, pressure groups and stakeholder groups and legislatures all are institutions.
  • There is a strong belief that philosophy, history and law have presented to the study of politics and it is in the field of institutional approaches.
  • So institutional approaches are ancient and important approach to the study of political science.
  • So these approaches mainly deals with the formal aspect of government and politics.
  • So institutional approach is concerned with the study of formal political structures like legislature, executive and judiciary.

Rules, Powers & the Narrow Focus on Formal Structures

  • In this manner we can say that it is focused on the rules of the political system, the powers of the various institutions and the legislative bodies and how the constitution worked.
  • So main drawback of this approach was its narrow focus on formal structures and arrangement only.
  • In a far-reaching term, an institution can be described as any persistent system of activities in any pattern of group behavior.
  • So more concentrately an institution has been regarded as office and agencies arranged in a hierarchy each agency having certain functions and powers.
  • The study of institution has been dominant not only to the arena of comparative politics but to the political science field also.
  • Many writers have argued that the institutions have shaped political behavior and social change and these authors have taken an institutionalist approach which treat institution as an independent variables.
  • The institutional approach to political analysis emphasizes on the formal structures and agencies of the government.
  • So it originally concentrated on the development and operation of the legislature, executive and judiciary.
  • As the approach developed, however, the list is extended to include political parties, constitutions, bureaucracy, interest group and other institutions which are enduringly engaged in the political system.
  • Traditional political thinkers were primarily interested in the activities and the roles of various types of institutions looking at politics from an institution perspective.
  • So political system studies the state or the government and are related to all the institutions.

Institutions in the Definition of Politics

  • This is also the definition of politics and therefore politics cannot be separated from the state or government and that is the institution.
  • There is another definition which emphasize on the institutional approach.
  • So the emphasis on the institutional and structural approaches lies almost exclusively in the formal aspect of the government and politics.
  • So it is also called the structural approach because of the various institution make up the structure of the society.
  • Though descriptive institutional approaches slightly old political expert still concentrate chiefly in scrutinizing the major political institution of the state such as the executive, legislature and the civil service, judiciary, local government and from these examination valuable insights as to their organization can be drawn. proposal for reform conversed and general conclusion obtainable.
  • Even after this approach has been critiqued for the disregard of informal aspect of the politics such as individual norms, social beliefs, cultural values, groups, attitudes, personality and the processes.

Criticism: Too Narrow & Formal

  • Institutional approach is also criticized for being too narrow and limited to only the formal structures.
  • It ignores the role of individuals who constitute and operate the formal as well as informal structures and substructure of the political system.
  • There is another problem that is the meaning and the range of an institutional system vary with the view of the scholars.
  • Researchers of this approach ignored the international political system as well.

Institutional vs Legal Approach

  • Institutional approach is closely related to the legal approach yet it is different.
  • Significantly this approach does not solely bank on other discipline like philosophy, history and law.
  • Amongst the traditional approaches, it alone gives independent identity to the systematic study of politics.
  • So in this manner, we can say that institutional approach focus on the study of political institution and structures like executive, legislature and judiciary with political parties, interest group etc.
  • According to the upholders of the institutional approach, it proceeded to the study of organization and functioning of the government, its various organs, political parties and other institutions affecting politics.
  • So there is a classification of governments starting from Aristotle as he discussed about six type of government like monarchy, tyranny, aristocracy, oligarchy, polity and democracy to the modern classification like democracy, dictatorship, parliamentary system, presidential, unitary and federal etc.
  • So it is a trans transition from the class classical to the modern form of government and the identification of the levels of government with composition and powers of each of these and their inter relationship between the other systems.
  • So there is a chief concern of this approach is to focus about the structures of all the political system and to study it in a deeper manner.

A Shift from Normative to Empirical Description

  • So it aims to giving an elaborate description to facts.
  • Hence it exemplifies a shift from normative to empirical approach and from the historical to the contemporary concern within the sphere of traditional approaches.
  • However, it relies heavily on description rather than the explanation.
  • Hence it fails to qualify as contemporary approach.
  • So we know that there is a strong belief that philosophy, history and law have been given the study of politics which falls in the field of institutional approaches.
  • The institutional approach is an old and important approach to the study of political science as it focus more on the formal structure of the organization.
  • These approaches primarily deals with the formal aspect of the government and politics.
  • So the institutional approach involves studying formal political structures such as legislature, executive and judiciary with some informal organization or structures like political parties, pressure groups and other organizations.

Drawback: Focus on Formal Structures

  • So it focused on the rules of the political system, the power of various institutions and the legislative body and how constitution work.
  • So the main drawback back of this approach was to focus on the formal structure and placement.
  • In broad terms, the system can be described in any permanent system of activity in any pattern of group behavior.
  • So more specifically, institutions are considered hierarchically arranged offices and agencies with each agency having a specific function and authority.
  • So institutional studies dominated not only the field of comparative politics but the entire field of political system.

Institutions Shaping Political Behaviour

  • Many writers claim that the system shaped political behavior and social change by evaluating the formal and informal political structures of the organization.
  • So these authors take an institutional approach that treats institution as independent variables and structures.
  • The institutional approach to political analysis focuses on the formal structure and institution of the government like legislative, executive and judiciary.
  • So, initially the focus was on the development and the operation of these important organs.
  • However, as the approach evolves, the list expand to include political parties, constitution, bureaucracy, stakeholder groups and other institutions or some other organization that are more or less permanently engaged or involved in the political system.
  • Although the descriptive institutional approach is a bit old, political experts are still primarily focused on scrutinizing the state’s major political institutions such as the executive branch, legislature, civil servant, judiciary, local government and by investigating these you can draw their organization, discuss form, proposal and draw general conclusions.
  • So it is very important to understand that institutional approach focus more on the formal structure of the political body where you can analyze them where you can observe and you can presume so many changes and you can also propose some different changes and reforms within the organization.
  • Not only does this approach has been criticized for ignoring informal aspect of the politics as you know that it this approach focus more on the formal structure of the organization or a political system like it focus on the important part of the political system like legislature, executive and judiciary.

Constitutional Part & Political Parties

  • For example the individual norms, social beliefs, cultural values, groups, attitudes, personalities and processes.
  • So this institutional approach has also been criticized for being too narrow because it has a very narrow aspect to study only the formal structure or the organization which are related to the political system only.
  • So it has a very limited kind of scope within the system.
  • It constitute and operates the formal and informal structures and substructure of the political system which ignores the role of individuals and it doesn’t talk about the political or individual participation in the political system.
  • It more focus on the institutions and structure of the political body.

Scope of the Institutional System Differs by Scholar

  • Researchers in this approach ignored international politics.
  • So it mainly focus on the domestic politics where it try to study the important structures formal and informal structure of the political system but due to this it ignores the international politics.

Legal Approach – Historical Overview

Video Part 1 · starts at 4:48:02

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • At the end of the session, you will be able to define the legal approach and its historical overview.

What Is the Legal Approach?

  • So before discussing anything first we will discuss what is legal approach.
  • Legal approach stands for an attempt just to understand politics in terms of law.
  • So it focuses its attention on the legal and constitutional framework in which different organs of the government have to function inquires into their respective legal position and their powers with procedures which make their action legally valid.
  • For instance, the legal approach to Indian politics will proceed to analyze legal implications of various provisions of the Indian Constitution duly documented by the decisions of the Supreme Court of India as well as the opinions of the legal luminaries, procedures and formation of legal position of the two houses of Indian Parliament and state legislature.
  • In the area of traditional approaches, there are legal approaches.
  • This approach consider the state to be the central body for law making and enforcement.
  • As with the example of Indian legal system and Indian political system, we have understood that the Supreme Court with other important organs are having the authority to take decision legally.
  • So we can say that this approach is associated with the legal processes, legal institutions and the judiciary.
  • In this approach, political research is mixed with the legal process and institutions.

Law & Justice in Political Science

  • So the theme of law and justice is not treated as mere jurisprudence.
  • But political scientists see the state as a maintainer of an effective and equitable system of law and order.
  • So the responsibility of a state is to maintain law and order within the society.
  • Matters concerning the organization, jurisdiction and the independence of judiciary are the essential concern of the political scientists within the society.
  • So this approach treats the state primarily as an organization or an as an institution for legislation and enforcement of the policies and law.
  • Proponents of this approach are Cicero, Bodin, Hobbes, John Austin, Dicey etc.
  • In Hob system, the head of the state is the supreme legal authority which is known as the sovereign and his orders are laws must be obeyed to avoid punishment after a breach or to keep away the dreaded state of nature.
  • So Hobbes was in a favor of a absolute sovereign who was heading the state with supreme legal authority.
  • Other social scientists have stated that political studies are linked at to national legal proceedings and that the existence of a harmonious state of freedom and equality.
  • So the focus of any state is to maintain a state of freedom and equality which is dictated and ruled by the rule of law.

Law in Domestic & International Politics

  • So the legal approach applies to domestic international politics.
  • It assumes that the law stipulate the actions to be taken in each contingency and prohibits the same in certain other situations.
  • It also emphasized the fact that when citizens comply with the law, knowledge of the law provides an important basis for prediction related to people’s political behavior.
  • So in this manner we can say that it is an effective approach but it is not without criticism. So this approach is very limited and narrow.
  • Also the law contains only one aspect of people’s life.

Limits of the Legal Approach

  • It is only possible and not to cover the entire political behavior.
  • So it is only related to the legal system of the state or political body.
  • Some writers say that the political development is primarily a function of the statutory constitution that governs functions such as equal protection under the law, the rule of law, secret ballot of election, federalism and the separation of power.
  • So the concept has been clearly implied in much of the formal political science studies of Europe and the United States over the last 100 years even if not explicitly stated.
  • So historical descriptive and legal forms of research remained the dominant concern of American political scientist until at least 1950s.
  • So the legal form of approach was less obvious in books about developing regions than in the United States and Western Europe.
  • The Latin America political literature perhaps changing embodies it to a greater extent that embodies it to a greater extent than studies in other developing regions.
  • In general, one of the most notable feature of political research in the developing region is how far the pendulum is from its legal form and explanatory variable to economic, administrative, sociological and psychological factors.
  • So with this note we can say that we have learned and defined the legal approach with its historical overview.

Legal Approach – Critical Evaluation

Video Part 1 · starts at 4:54:18

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • At the end of this session you will be able to define the legal approach and its critical evaluation.

Legal & Constitutional Framework

  • So legal approach as you know it stands for understanding the politics in terms of law.
  • So its focus is on legal and constitutional framework in which different organs of the government have to function.
  • They have to inquire into their respective legal position, their powers and the procedures which make their action legally valid.
  • Moreover, all political processes to become effective and stable must culminate.
  • Moreover, under the legal system, all the political processes to become effective and stable must culminate in legal provisions.
  • Whether it is an independence movement in a colonized country or it’s an agitation for civil rights or certain concessions for any sections of the societies.
  • Besides the study of constitutional law and international law, in spite of its limited use in understanding politics, continues to play a pal role in the social and political life of almost every country.

The State as Creator & Enforcer of Law

  • So we know it very well that legal approach regards state as a creator and enforcer of law and deals with the legal system and institution with its processes.
  • So in this manner we can say that the legal approach represents an attempt to understand politics from a legal point of view.
  • It draws attention to the legal and constitutional frameworks that various government agencies must function in and investigates their respectives like legal positions for legally validating their actions.
  • Number two, powers are legally validating their actions and at last procedures for legally validating their actions.

Legal Position, Powers & Procedures

  • So within the legal approach, we focus on the legal position, powers and procedures for validating the actions of the political system.
  • For instance, India’s legal approach to policy makes it’s possible to analyze the legal implication of various provisions of the Indian constitution.
  • This comprises the judgment of the Supreme Court of India as well as the opinions of the saturated This comprises the judgment of the Supreme Court of India as well as the opinions of the other bodies like the procedure for formation.
  • The two houses of the Indian state like parliament and the state legislature, election or appointment procedures, president, prime minister, government or the governor with state cabinet.
  • They are playing a very crucial role and having the authority of the Supreme Court of India with high court and full legal implication of the establishment of federal government. the position of basic rights and guiding principles of national policy.

Legal Approaches to International Policy

  • Similarly, legal approaches to international policy tend to analyze its principally in accordance with the requirement of international law.
  • Moreover, all political processes for effectiveness and stability, whether it is an independence movement in a colonized country or an insentiment of civil rights are at specific concessions of every part of the society. Regardless, legal provisions need to culminate.
  • In addition, research on the constitution and international law continues to play a vital role in the social and political life of almost every country despite its limited use in understanding politics.
  • The legal approach treats the state as the creator and enforcer of the law and deals with the legal system and processes.
  • So it supports the include its supporters included Cicero, Bodin, Thomas Hobbes, Jeremy Bentham, John Austin, Dicey etc.
  • In the area of traditional approaches, there are legal approaches and this approach considers the state to be a central body for lawmaking and enforcement and therefore this approach is associated with legal processes, legal institutions and judiciary.
  • In this approach, political research is mixed with legal processes and institutions.

Law & Justice as Political Science

  • So the theme of law and justice is not treated as mere a jurisprudence issue but political scientists see the state as a maintainer of an effective and equitable justice and system of law with order.
  • So matters concerning the organization jurisdiction and the independence of judiciary are of the essential concern of political scientist.
  • This approach treats the state primarily as an organization for legislation and enforcement of the policies by the political body.
  • As we have discussed the proponent of this approach, now we can say that the head of the state according to the hopes is supreme legal authority which actually explains the supreme body as a sovereign or absolute body of the political system. in his orders and laws that must be obeyed to avoid punishment after the breach or to keep away the dreaded state of the nature.
  • So Hobbes in his natural condition with the social contract theory explained the absolute sovereign king or the authority who was heading the state with supreme legal authority.
  • Other scientists have also stated the political studies which are linked to national legal proceedings and that the existence of a harmonious state of freedom and equality which is dictated by the rule of law.
  • So in this manner we can say that the legal approach applies to domestic and international politics.
  • It is also based on the assumption that the law stipulates the action to be taken in a given contingency and also prohibits the same in certain other situations.

Citizens’ Compliance & Criticism

  • So it also emphasizes the fact that when citizens comply with law, knowledge of the law provides an important basis for prediction related to people’s political behavior.
  • So it is an effective approach but it’s not without criticism.
  • This approach is very narrow and having a very limited scope.
  • The law contains only one aspect of people’s life.
  • It is not possible to cover the entire behavior of political people or political behavior of the individual.

  • Now we’ll try to understand the political behavior with the economic approach which is shaped by economy.

Institutions & Political Motivation

Ideologies of Political Economy

Marxism on Inequality & Private Ownership

  • Therefore, political economy provides us an understanding of how countries and households are managed and governed by considering both the political and economic factors associated with each other.

Political Economy Approach – Critical Evaluation

Video Part 1 · starts at 5:09:24

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • At the end of the session, you will be able to understand political economic approach and its critical evaluation.

Changing Meanings of ‘Political Economy’

  • So over its long lifetime the phrase political economy has had many different meanings.
  • For Adam Smith political economy was the science of managing the nation’s resources as so to generate wealth.
  • And for Marx it was how the ownership of the means of production influenced historical processes.
  • For much of the 20th century, the phrase political economy has contradictory meanings. Sometimes it was viewed as an area of study.
  • The interreationship between the economics and politics while at other times it was viewed as methodological approach.
  • Even the methodological approach was divided into two parts. the economic approach which is sometimes called public choice and sometimes called positive political theory.
  • On the other hand, it is also emphasizing the individual rationality and the another approach is the sociological approach where the level of analysis tended to be institutional only.
  • So we have discussed two approaches economic approach and the sociological approach in the methodological approach of political and economic approach.

Political Economy as a Grand Synthesis

  • Here we view political economy as a grand synthesis of various trends.
  • In our view, political economy is a methodology of economics applied to the analysis of political behavior and institutions only.
  • As such, it is not a single unified approach but a family of approaches because institutions are not longer ignored but instead they are themselves the subject matter of the investigation.
  • So this approach incorporates many of the issues concerned related to political sociologist because political behavior and institutions are subject of study.
  • Politics also become the subject of political economy.
  • So all of this tied together by a set of methodology typically associated with economics but now part and parcel of political science itself.

Production, Trade & Economic Systems

  • So political economy is a social science that studies production, trade and their relationship with the law and government.
  • So it is a study of how economic theory affects various social economic systems such as socialism, communism and the development and implementation of public policies.
  • There are different groups of economies have different beliefs about how the economy should develop and therefore political economy is a complex area that covers a wide range of political interest.
  • Simply put, political economy refers to the advice given by economics of the government on either general economic policies or specific proposal made by the politicians.

Components of Political Economy

  • There are few components of political economy.
  • So there are two categories or two sections of the components related to political economy.
  • Number one is the classical political economy and another one is the modern political economy.
  • Classical economics studies the works of philosophers such as Macawi, Adam Smith and Karl Marx.
  • On the other hand, modern political economics studies the achievement of modern philosophers, economists and political scientists.
  • Political economy studies are influenced by game theory because they include various groups competing for finite resources and power to assess which policies produce the most beneficial results.

Economic Capacity to Achieve Results

  • So it is also related to the economic capacity to achieve the desired results.
  • So in this manner we can say that political economy research focuses on three major areas. Number one is the interdisciplinary study.
  • From an interdisciplinary perspective, political economy focuses on economics, sociology and political science to understand how economic system, political institution and the environment influence each other. So there are three areas included in it. One is economic models of political process.
  • Number two, international political economy and how it affects international relations.
  • And number three is the resource allocation in various economic system.
  • Is it a private allocation or a public allocation?

The New Political Economy

  • New political economy.
  • So the new field of political economy treats economic policy as a belief or action that needs further discussion not as a framework that needs to be analyzed.
  • So it connects the ideology of classical economics with the new advance in the field of political economy.
  • So this approach aims to dismiss the old ideals of the government, state and market interest and facilitate political debate about the needs and requirement of the society. International political economy.
  • International political economy studies also known as global political economy analyzes the relationship between the economics and international relations.
  • So we use ideas from economics, sociology and politics to understand the international political economy which focus on how states and institutions use global economic interaction just to form political system.

Modernization Theory

Video Part 1 · starts at 5:15:22

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • At the end of this session you will be able to understand what is modernization theory and how it works within the society.

Rise of Modernisation Theory in the 1950s–60s

  • Modernization theory refers to a body of theory that became prominent in 1950s and60s in relation of understanding issues of economic and social development and in creating policies that would assist economic and social transition in the poorer countries.
  • So there are various components of modernization theory received critiques from the outset but their influence within the policymaking endured for a significant period of time.
  • So it is a transition from the traditional approach to the modern approach.
  • The set of theories that tried to interpret the process of modernization in the 19s and 50s was by no means coherent set of ideas.
  • Rather it constituted a wide variety of theories that emerged from a range of social science discipline as well as countries and universities.
  • Modernization theory cast development as a uniform evolutionary route that all societies follow from agriculture, rural and traditional societies to postindustrial, urban and modern forms.
  • In other words, all society once engaged in modernization process follow a predetermined sequence of development stages.
  • For example, traditional economies, transition to take off, take off itself, drive to maturity, age of high consumption and pre-industrial societies.
  • So we can say that modernization theory emphasizes the internal forces and sources of socioeconomic development such as formal education, market-based economy and democratic secular political process.

Overcoming the Errors of Traditional Approaches

  • So there are political philosophers who realize the need to look at politics from a new point of view.
  • Thus in order to overcome from the errors of the traditional approaches and a variety of new approaches were promoted by new political intellectuals.
  • So this new approach are seen as modern approaches in the study of political science.
  • So for many philosophers and scholars such approaches are a reaction of a traditional approaches and these approaches are primarily concerned with the science of policy and the first innovation in the respect was introduction of the behavioral revolution in political science.
  • There are few traits of modern approaches as like these approaches draw conclusion based on the subjective evidence.
  • These approaches transcend the study of political structure and their historical analysis.
  • Modern methods believe in interdisciplinary study.
  • They emphasize scientific research method and try to draw scientific conclusion in political science.

Technology, Development & External Influences

  • Although modernization theory does not root out external forces in the sources of social change and economic development but it focuses less on foreign influences. There are some among external influences.
  • However, science is exceptional because it is considered beneficial to developing countries by way of knowledge and technology transfer from the developed countries.
  • In other words, we can say that societies can be fast track to modernization by importing western technical capital forms of organization, science and technology to developing countries.
  • There is one important thinker like W. W. Rostow who argues the adoption of scientific methods and scientific ways of thinking and acquisition of technoscientific skills which are critical at the transition of takeoff stage of development.
  • So essentially proponents of modernization theory view science and technology and catalyst for development.
  • Science and technology provide conducive environment for the economic growth in the developing countries through their ability to provide rational protocols in decision making for the efficient use of material and human resources.
  • So as far as modernization is concerned development is simply a matter of knowledge and technology transfer that is unproatic and straightforward contextf free and nondisruptive for existing social structure and cultural arrangement in the developing countries.

Levy & the Unilinear Model: Eurocentric Critique

  • Levy, another early theorist who wrote in 1960s argued that as the level of modernization increased, so did the structural uniformity between societies.
  • So this unilinear model has been adopted since the revival of modernization theory in the 1990s.
  • So the two main criticism of Rostow’s modernization model are that it is Eurocentric and teleological.
  • Jack Goldstone argues that the heroic story of the rise of the west is overemphasized in modernization theory.

Multiple Modernities

  • He argues that many early modern and non-European societies have experienced a wind of economic growth and a steady increase in technological change.
  • He also challenges Britain exceptionalism by arguing that Britain’s industrial success is a historical inconsistency that stem from the lucky link between economic blossoming and growing engineering culture.
  • So the enlightenment that all society inevitably develop a similar set of ideas, customs and institutions is a simplification.
  • Adam Smith an early influential figure in modernization theory used the framework of fourstep theory of to condense all societies into one homogeneous form.
  • Another one is the Sanjay Subrahmanyam who discussed about the different categories of modernization and they tend to use categories derived from European experiences which shape the question asked by a comparative historian.
  • So western modernity has given a privileged status and is a set of inferior standards for all other societies.
  • Another enlightenment philosopher who discussed about Eurocentric ideas and he believed that other parts of the world could look the western European societies for growth and development.
  • So the modern rubric is rooted in the idea of European dominance only.
  • Moreover, historians are now aware that there are many different destinations as well as many roads to the present day.

Societies’ Different Paths to Modernisation

  • So the concept of multiple modernity help to undermine the credibility of the notion that modernization is a synonym with westernization.
  • Some thinkers argue that the form of modernization of society as a whole is not homogeneous due to its diverse cultural and historical backgrounds.
  • So the clear vision of fundamentalist movement is formulated in terms of common to modernity discourse and they attempted to make modernity appropriate in their own terms only to who states the East Asian modernity is more focused on soft authoritarianism patriarchal regimes and government leadership in the market economy.
  • So there are two thinkers to and shuml who prove the modernity is not a uniform and does not only come from the western Europe only.
  • So this adaption succeed in addressing the teleology and Eurocentrism of the original theory but it is at the risk of being too broad to lose its core meaning and thus become written in it.
  • So we can say that adaption of modernization theory has helped to redefine the modernity means and they question the rubrics we choose to follow and help us to understand alternative economic and political trajectories.

Development, Underdevelopment & Dependency Theory

Video Part 1 · starts at 5:24:00

🎯 Learning Objectives

Dependency vs Western Approaches to Development

  • So dependency theory differs from the most western approaches to studying political development.
  • As you can see two terms development and underdevelopment.
  • So you can also understand the difference between the development or underdevelopment.
  • And because of that this you can see the progress you can see the difference between the growth you can see the difference between their situation circumstances and conditions also.
  • So that’s why another theory evolved as a critique of modernization theory and this is dependency theory which differs from the most western approaches to studying political development.
  • So third world subordinate thinkers were interested in explaining the unequal and unjustified s circumstances in which they and their nations found themselves.
  • So they realize that their conditions are very different.
  • They are having different circumstances and due to this their progress level is also different.

Rich & Poor Countries

  • The countries of the third world were poor and the developed countries were rich and this is one of the major difference between the developed and underdeveloped or developing countries.
  • Third world countries were in poor health and while other countries were in good health and the countries of the third world had little military power.
  • On the other hand, those countries who are developed countries, they are having more military resources.
  • So, third world countries faced hunger citizens were not in a good condition.
  • Their social, economic and political conditions were very much distorted and disturbed.
  • Due to this, you can see the difference between the development and underdevelopment.
  • There are other issues like third world economy was monoproductive and agriculture-based and the economies of the developed countries were diversified and industrialized and due to almost all traditional socio economic measures.
  • Third world countries were at the bottom of the scale and they had little education, wealth, health, military power and were politically economically dominated by the first world countries also.
  • And in this picture you can see the condition of almost all the underdeveloped countries and their situations.

Inequality: The Central Question

  • Dependency is a one of the most important theory which talks about the development side of the underdeveloped countries.
  • So dependency theory theorist asked why such inequality exist.
  • Their central concern was to understand the causes of inequality.
  • They felt such inequality was unjust and tried to explain it in order to change them and to achieve their goal of increasing equality between the state and the people.
  • So dependency theory is always quite controversial as it incorporates some Marxist concept condemned equality and inequality in the developed countries and address the delicate issues of inequality within the society. It originates in the third world countries.

Core Propositions of Dependency Theory

  • There are major proposition of dependency theory.
  • But before this first we will discuss what is dependency theory all about.
  • So dependency theory is the notion that resource flow from periphery of poor and underdeveloped state to a core of wealthy states enriching the later at the expense of the former.
  • So it is a central contention of dependency theory that poor states are empowered and rich ones are enriched by the way poor states are integrated into the world system.
  • So this theory was officially developed in the late 1960s following the world war second and scholars searched the roots issues in the lack of development in the Latin American states.
  • So the theory arose as a reaction to modernization theory and earlier theory of development which held that all societies progress through similar stages of the development that today’s underdeveloped areas are thus in a similar situation to that of the today’s developed areas at some time in the past and that therefore the task of helping the underdeveloped areas out of poverty is the to accelerate them along this supposed common path of development by various means such as investment technology transfers and closer integration into the world market.
  • But dependency theory rejected this view arguing that underdeveloped countries are not merely primitive versions of the developed countries but they have unique features and structure of their own and importantly are in situation of being weaker members in the world market economy also.
  • So this by this you can understand the dependency theory and now we will discuss the major proposition of dependency theory.

Third World Countries in the World Economy

  • Third world countries do not exist in isolation.
  • They can only be understood in the context of world’s economic and political system.
  • Political events in the third world countries are directly related to the events of the first world countries.
  • In the world of political economy system, there is an tremendous amount of interaction between the core countries and their people and between the core of its surroundings. So politics and economy are interrelated.
  • They cannot be understood apart from each other.
  • So economic ties and relationship between the core and neighboring countries are especially very important for each other.
  • From point three we can see that underdevelopment is not a natural condition but a caused condition.

Core–Periphery & Underdevelopment

  • In fact, developed countries are actively developing third world countries as a result of the system of interaction between them.
  • In other words, we can say that the underdevelopment of the weak third world countries is directly related and enables the development of the strong countries of industrialized core.
  • Both the central and peripheral parts are a part of the world’s political economy system. neither of which could exist without each other.
  • Moreover, as long as capitalism remains the world’s major economic system, there is no reason for situation in the developed and developing countries to change.
  • The global relations system is replicated in individuals third world countries. there is a core area that control the uses of parameter of the country.
  • So the center of the country’s economic, political, cultural and military powers lie in the center of the country and as a result of contact and interaction between the two regions.
  • The power and wealth of the center grows faster than inside.
  • So in this sense we can say that the national leaders of the capital exploit people for their own personal gain and power also.
  • As a result these domestic leaders could actually be conceptualized as agents of the international system.

Solutions Proposed by Dependency Theorists

  • But there are so many solutions for this theory.
  • Dependency theorists not only provide a conceptual framework for analyzing third world politics but also propose some solution to the central problem of inequality.
  • So the range of solution is wide as there is so much diversity among dependent theorist.
  • One suggestion is the formation of a common market trade block or keral.
  • So the idea is that third world countries share many common economic and trade issues in relation to industrialized core.
  • The second suggestion is to improve the situation which is which they have the elite of the world countries confront and take voluntary step to change their dependence.
  • More radical dependent theorists are looking for revolutionary changes and solution.
  • So they argue that it is unrealistic to expect people in power to take voluntary action that are personally unfavorable.
  • So in conclusion, it should be noted that the location of the dependency is basically the status quo.
  • Dependent theorist argued that the existing domestic and international economic and political systems are responsible for their injustice and inequality.

Radical Change Instead of Stability

  • They are seeking systematic change to solve the problems and they want a sudden nonlinear radical change.
  • Instead of supporting and accepting stability, they seek radical changes within the system.
  • So the perception analytical approaches and solutions differ significantly from the diffusion or order approach theorist.
  • So stability is the solution for order theorist and stability is a problem for dependence theorist also.

World System Analysis

World System Perspective

  • World system theory or also known as world system analysis and world system perspective which is a multi-disciplinary approach to world history and social change.
  • And this theory emphasized the world system as the primary unit of social analysis.
  • In this manner we can say that world system refer to the inter regional and transnational division of labor which divides the world into core countries, semipariperic countries and periphery countries.
  • So core countries focus on higher skill capital intensive production and the rest of the world focuses on lower skill labor intensive production and extraction of raw material.
  • This constantly reinforce the dominance of the core countries.
  • However, this system has dynamic characteristics in as a part of result of revolution in transport technology. individual states can gain and lose their core status over time.
  • So this structure is unified by division of labor and it is world economy rooted in a capitalist economy.
  • For a time certain countries become world hegemony.
  • During the law during the last few centuries as the world system has extended geographically and intensified economically this status has passed from some countries to the other countries.

Components of World System Analysis

  • So there are some components of the world system analysis but before this we will discuss this in detail.
  • So world system analysis as I said it is an approach to understanding social change based on geological history system.
  • So they provide a space-time framework for understanding social change that replaces the orthodox use of nation state as the basic unit of change.
  • The geological history system shows a specific structure of social relationship that is concretely realized through time and space.
  • So the modern world system is capitalist world economy.
  • The basic geological history is that it built in the long Europe of the 16th century spread all over the time about 1900 and disappeared in the 21st century.
  • Looking at the changes in the society in this way we can see that the basic driving force of the system is constant accumulation of capitalism.
  • So this dominant process of social change creates a particular time and space and this approach of the social sciences was pioneered by Immanuel Wallerstein in 1970s taking advantage of the experience of first world of the 1968 revolution which spurred the orthodox old left and the third world experience of severe constraint on the national liberation revolution.
  • Wallerstein challenged two great geographical myths of the time directly.

Developmentalism & Wallerstein’s Critique

  • Developmentalism.
  • The world is divided into developed countries and developing countries. Cold war thinking.
  • So the world is divided into capitalist and communist countries on the basis of their ideologies.
  • For rollerstein, both development and the economic system are characteristics of the entire historic system, not individual countries.

Countering the Myths of Developmentalism

  • World system analysis was dei devised to counter these myths. How does this approach interpret today’s world?
  • So the early 21st century is very rare, exciting and unstable period for two very different reasons.
  • The conclusion and the hegemony cycle are in agreement causing great normal stress on the system which is economic restructuring and political reordering.
  • There is a more important match for a symptomatic tendencies that are approaching their limits to cause an abnormal crisis in the system.
  • All geological history system are coming to an end and we are now at the end of the modern world system.

Globalisation & the Transition to a New World System

  • Also, Wall Street called this era of transition to a new unknown world system.
  • It challenges the enlightenment belief in social progress as inevitable feature of social change. an idea translated by socialist into labor advancement.
  • There is a relationship between world system analysis and globalization.
  • How are these two discourses about social change linked to one another?
  • In simple term, globalization advocates treating global system analyst as pioneers of global research but rejecting system frameworks.
  • Meanwhile, global system analysts consider globalization pro proponents refuse to trust the market. Of course, both denials are very basic.

Contrasting Pedigrees of World Systems & Modernisation

  • In some respect, it is difficult to imagine two approaches to understanding modern political and social changes that may be different within the society.
  • In the first place, they have a completely contrasting pedigree.
  • One is a coherent academic block of social science knowledge and other is a collection of miscellaneous text dominated by a business masters.
  • So this is reflected in the respective politics neo Marxism versus neoliberalism and later neoconservatism.
  • However, we can make useful comparison in terms of their similarity. There are three important similarities.
  • Both the approaches are multistractual but focus on the economy only.
  • Both aim to explain macrosocial changes based on materialistic understanding of social change.
  • And both the approaches place the state at the central stage of analysis but downgrade it as analytical category or a political category.
  • Neither proposes a multissectoral alternative to the state but both are slightly related to the literature on cities around the world.

State-Centred Approach

Video Part 1 · starts at 5:41:20

🎯 Learning Objectives

What Is the State-Centred Approach?

  • Now just find out what a state centered approach.
  • State centered theory or approach is a political theory which stresses the role of the government on civil society.
  • So it holds that the state itself can structure political life to some degree or some extent but does not facilitate the way power is distributed between the classes and other groups at a given time.
  • So this theory holding that the national government represent a voluntary compact or agreement between the states which retain a dominant position.
  • Supporters of the state centered federalism included Thomas Jefferson and the republican party and they saw constitution as an agreement among the states of which gave them the ability to self-governance.
  • There are national policy makers who interact with economically who interact with economy independently with intention of raising social welfare and this gives legitimacy to market intervention.
  • In the field of comparative political economy, there was a backlash against developmentalism in the late 1960s and early 1970s when the concept of state and power revived.
  • Contribution to national theory came primarily from Marxist scholars.

Marx, Engels & Lenin: State & Class

Globalization & the Neoliberal Approach

Video Part 1 · starts at 5:47:59

🎯 Learning Objectives

Neoliberalism: Deregulation & Privatisation

  • Neoliberalism or neoliberal approach is a term used to describe the 20th century resurgence of 19th century ideas associated with free market capitalism.
  • It is a significant factor in the rise of conservatism and libertarian organization political parties and think tanks and predominantly advocated by them.
  • So it is generally associated with the policies of economic liberalization including privatization, deregulation, globalization, free trade and reduction in the government spending in order to increase the role of private sector in the economy and society.
  • However, the defining feature of neoliberalism is both thought and practice have been the subject of substantial scholarly debate.

Competing Definitions of Neoliberalism

  • So the term has multiple competing definition and peorative wells in policymaking neoliberalism often refers to what was part of the paradigm shift that followed the alleged failure of another economies in the developing and developed countries.
  • According to neoliberalism, big government and too much public development assistance hinder economic and social development while deregulation, privatization and tax cuts are needed to achieve economic growth.
  • And this post outlines the development of a neoliberal approach and then briefly evaluates the effectiveness of a new liberal policies.
  • The use of term neoliberalism is a quite different but here we will use the term to refer a series of economic policies that have prevailed in economic development over the last three decades since the late 1980s namely increased privatization.
  • Number two is economic deregulation and third one which is tax cuts.
  • So neoliberalism replaced modernization theory as a formal approach to development in the 1980s.
  • It focuses on economic policies and institution that are considered to be restraining development in order to limit the free market.

The Washington Consensus, the World Bank & the IMF

  • The agreement between the World Bank and the IMF that neoliberal policy is the best path to development is called the Washington consensus.
  • After the world leaders met in Washington in 1989, the predominant form of globalization is neoliberal globalization.
  • According to critiques, neoliberal policy aims to create an economic framework that allows investment cost to be minimized, social security to be reduced, profits to be made by preaching individualism.
  • And with the rise of neoliberalism, they argue that society as a whole is increasingly dominated and primitated by economic logic.
  • So the logic of commodities and accumulated financial capital.
  • Neoliberalism is often associated with the following features.

Withdrawal of the State & Decline of the Welfare State

  • The state withdraws from all areas of social life.
  • The welfare state and collective responsibility are destroyed.
  • Self-help, individual self- responsibility for problems and the ability of the market to regulate themselves without human interventions are described.
  • Growth, productivity, and competitions are presented as the sole goals of human behavior.
  • Neoliberalism is often also associated with s certain other features like old ultra liberal ideas are presented as modern and progressive.
  • Money and financial markets are homogenized under the control of several countries.

Individualisation of Labour & Market Competition

  • Some new social dronism convey the message that only strong and remarkable ones survive in the society and the market. permanent anxiety about wages and living conditions.
  • So individualization of labor contracts, states support the state subsidies for large corporation are all established and institutionalized.
  • The neoliberal ideology is that the economy is independent of society.
  • Markets are best way to organize production and distribution efficiently and equitably. and the globalization needs to minimize national spending especially on social security.
  • So these developments are presented as unavoidable, self-evident and irreplaceable.

Flexible Labour Laws

  • So neoliberal states create a legal framework for classive.
  • So neoliberal states create a legal framework for flexible wages and flexible working hours.
  • So collective bargaining systems are increasingly being replaced by sector regional and enterprise level systems.
  • In this system, the state seeks to derive capital investment and technological process through subsidies, research and development, R&D programs, funding and institutional support.
  • Increasingly the state is creating new forms of independence and self-employment like reducing unemployment benefits and welfare, tightening qualification standards and introducing sanctions and compulsory activation programs for labor and their welfare.

Pensions & Retirement Age

  • Further pension have been reduced and retirement ages have been raised. Private pension funds are encouraged.
  • Universities are considered enterprises and corporation between universities and enterprises which is encouraged by the cooperation between the different universities.
  • So here regulation will become increasingly important at the superstate regional and local levels shifting to them by establishing networks or links between cities, regions and federal states.
  • On the other hand, the functioning of the certain state is shifting to civil society.
  • Public enterprises and services are becoming more and more privatized and commercialized and welfare is increasingly shifting from private sector to the corporate level.
  • So the crossborder business are also introducing increasingly flexible ways to produce goods and they themselves are organized as globally dispersed companies that are political and economic actors within the society.
  • The nation state transform into a competitive nation.
  • There is a competition between nation states for good conditions of economic investment and therefore nation states are often privatized and deregulated.
  • Further capital and multinational corporation operate at the global level but states are forced to enforce political action at the national level.

Systems Approach

Video Part 1 · starts at 5:56:19

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • At the end of this session you will be able to understand the systems approach.

The Systems Approach in Comparative Politics

  • So what is system approach all about and what is the role of system approach in comparative politics?
  • Now let’s find out considering the development of all inclusive and universal or abstract theory as unattainable by empirical methods.
  • So some political scientists have adopted the limited perspective of systems theory.
  • Several disciplines have contributed to its making the development and it represent interdisciplinary nature of modern political science systems concept makes empirical and comparative study possible even of those political institution apart from the state such as international political system, city, political parties etc.
  • So the concept is very helpful in studying the changes like transformation, feedback, exchange, tension, conflict and development.
  • Besides some thinkers like Easton, Talcott Parsons, Carl, Morton, some other thinkers also studied system approach.
  • System theory analysis interaction structures, institutions and processes pertaining to politics.
  • So it analyzes the interaction and structures of the various institutions within the politics.
  • Politics involves power, authority, physical coercion and allocation of values for society.
  • So we can say that in all shade of politics, political processes and structures are enmeshed with several other elements, factors and considerations.

The Political System & Its Environment

  • As such, a political system cannot be physically separated from its non-political aspects and it is therefore usually understood and studied in the analytical manner only.
  • Society as a whole makes up the general political system which contains many subsystem and political system is one of these subsystem.
  • When the political system is studied as a whole along with its intra subsystem then it is treated as a system.
  • So besides the system can be considered as a part of environment and thus the concept of system both in interlocking micro and macro forms saves us from the error of considering system as isolated separate or independent entities.
  • So in this manner we can say that this approach is classified as a modern approach.
  • The concept of subsystem theory came out with the anti antiquity in the 1920s.
  • Ludwig is the considered the oldest supporter of the theory general system.

From Biology to General Systems Theory

  • So he used the theory as a part of his biology study and after the world war II sociologist emphasized the fusion of science with the aid of system theory.
  • But when the theories of general system in the abstract form revert to the natural sciences like biology, they are found in anthropology in the form of their manipulation.
  • After that, it was accepted by sociology and psychology.
  • During the 1960s, system theory became an important tool to the evaluation of study of key elements of political science.
  • David Easton was the first political scientist to apply this theory to policy analysis.
  • This approach describes the relationship between political life and other aspect of social life.
  • So the idea of system was borrowed from biology by Talcott Parsons who first promoted the concept of social systems and later David Easton further developed the concept of the political system.
  • So this approach meant that the political system was functioning in a social environment and therefore it is impossible to analyze political events separately from other aspects of the society.
  • In other words, social influences whether economic, religious or otherwise shape the political processes. The political system works in the environment.

Demands from the Environment

  • So the environment creates demand from different part of the society such as the demand for reservation for employment in a particular group.
  • The demand for calm working conditions or minimum wages, the demand for better transportation facilities, the demand for better health facilities.
  • So different request have different levels of support.
  • Easton said that both request and support establish input.
  • So the political system received these inputs from the environment and after considering various factors the government decides to take action on some of these demands but no other demands will be fulfilled through the conversion process.
  • Input is converted to output by decision makers in the form of policies, decisions, rules, regulations and laws.
  • So the output flow back into the environment through the feedback mechanism creating new demand.

A Recurring Process: Behavioural Systems Thinking

  • Therefore this is a recurring process to the today the term political system is chosen as the term state or government because it involves both formal and informal political directives and processes that continue to exist in society.
  • So the behavioral school systematic approach to the political system has evolved as a new concept and David Easton Gabriel Almond Morton Kaplan is praised for applying this approach to political science.
  • According to this theory, political behavior is considered as a system which is clearly defined as the authoritative distribution of threatening value and for the actual use of deprivation to restrain everything.
  • It is a system of interaction found in independent societies that perform integrated and adaptive functions both internally and externally through the adoption of legitimate physical impulses.
  • However, these features of a political system are openness, adaptiveness, comprehensiveness, self regulating and it is an ongoing process.
  • So it consists of many structures that have specific functions and these functions are pigeon holes as inputs and output functions.
  • So the political system does these to maintain itself.

The Individual as Voter, Leader & Party Member

  • So it concentrate on the individual as voter, leader, revolutionary, party member and the influence of group or political system on the individual’s political behavior.
  • So overall this approach tried to understand the actions or behavior of the individual in a political system.
  • Behavioralism firmly emphasized the scientific objective and worthless study of environmentally conditioned political events and the behavior of individuals involved in the phenomena.
  • So we can say that it focus more on the scientific objective and birthful part of the environment which is very crucial and important part of the society and therefore it focuses on the role of individual behavior and scientific analysis at various levels.
  • Behaviorism is the development of methods for traditional politics that did not take into account human behavior as an actor in politics.

Behaviorism & Stimulus–Response

  • Behaviorism refers to the reaction of an organism caused by some stimulus.
  • It does not consider the role of emotions, thoughts, prejudice that determine the individual’s reaction.
  • So it takes into account the roles of emotions, ideas and prejudices.
  • David Easton distinguished between activism and activism as usual.
  • So according to him the paradigm adopted by behavioral scholar is SR stimulus response.
  • However the action list was improved by making it S. It means stimulus organism responses.

Easton & the Behavioural Revolution

  • David Easton believes that the behavioral revolution is the intellectual tendency of political scientist to empirically study a person’s political behavior.
  • There are few striking features of behavioralism.
  • David Easton described certain important features of behavioralism that are considered its intellectual foundation and they are regularities.
  • Number one which is one of the most important feature of behavioralism is regularity.
  • And this approach believes that there is some coherence in the political behavior that can be expressed in generalization or theory in order just to elucidate and predict political phenomena.
  • In certain situation the political behavior of an individual can be more or less similar.
  • Such regularity of behavior may help researchers just to analyze political situations and predict future political phenomena.
  • So the study of such regularity makes political science more scientific and gives some predictions also. Another important feature is verification.

Verification & Systematisation

  • Activist do not want to accept everything that has been established.
  • So they have to verify every fact and findings and therefore they emphasize testing and verifying every data according to them.
  • If the phenomena is not verified, it is not scientific. Techniques. Another important feature is techniques.

Research Tools & Sophisticated Methods

  • So behavioral scientists focus on the use of these research tools and methods just to generate valid and reliable comparative data.
  • So researchers need to take advantage of sophisticated tools such as simple surveys, mathematical models and simulations.
  • Quantification is known as one of the most important feature of behavioral approach.
  • After collecting data, researchers need to measure and quantify those data also.
  • Not only this, we can include some other features like values, systematization, pure science and integration. So now we’ll focus on values.
  • Behavioral scholars have emphasized the separation of facts from the values.
  • So they believe that in order to do objective research, it must be worthless.
  • That means researchers should not have prejudices. Systematization.

Research & Theory Together

  • According to behavioral scientist, political science research must be systematic. Theory and research should go together only. Pure science.
  • Another feature of activism was the purpose of making political science as a pure science.
  • And this theory believes that political science research should be verified by evidence integration.
  • So behavioral scientists said that the political science should not be separated from various other social sciences such as history, sociology and economics.
  • So this approach shows that the political events are formed by various other factors within the society and therefore it is incorrect to separate political science from other disciplines.
  • So in this manner we can say that we need to focus more on the facts and we have to separate the facts from value.
  • With this we can also have kind of systematization and we can make it by this political science or other systems as a pure science with integration of certain subjects or other disciplines within the sub same political system.
  • As a result with the development of activism new thinking and new research methods have evolved in the field of political science.

Advantages of the Behavioural Approach

  • So the advantage of behavioral approaches are this approach creates political science as a scientific methods and seeks to bring it closer to the daily life of the individual.
  • So not only it is focusing on the formal structures and the functions of the political system, it is also talking about the individual behavior which is influencing the political system. So it is a combination of two functionality.
  • One is the formal functionality and another is the individual behavior through which you can overall understand the political system. activism has brought it means the behavioral activism has brought human behavior into the field of political science.
  • So thereby making research more relevant within the society and this approach helps predict future political events also.
  • So by analyzing the behavior of the individual, their political actions, their reactions and by understanding the structural function of the whole political system, you can also predict the future of any political event.

Structural-Functional Approach

🎯 Learning Objectives

Society as an Interrelated System: Inputs & Outputs

  • According to this approach, the society is a single interrelated system where each part of subsystem has a definite and distinct role to play.
  • So we can say that it talks about different separated structures within the society and every system is having its own separate function.
  • So the structural functional approach may be considered as an offshot of the system analysis and these approaches accenture the structures and functions of the society.
  • Gabriel Almond was one of the important advocate of this approach.

Political System as a System of Interaction

  • He prescribed political system as a special system of interaction that exist in all societies and performing certain functions.
  • According to him, the main in according to him the main attributes of a political system are comprehensiveness, interdependence and the existence of boundaries.
  • Like Easton, Almond also believed that all political system perform input and output functions.
  • So the input functions of political systems are political socialization and recruitment, interest articulation, interest aggression and political communication.
  • Gabriel Almond makes three-fold classification of governmental output functions relating to policy making and implementation and these output functions are rule making, rule application and rule adjudication and rule adjudication.
  • Therefore, Gabriel Almond believes that a stable and efficient political system converts inputs into the outputs.
  • So in this manner we can say that Easton and Almond believe that the political system perform input and out functions and these are political socialization and recruitment clarification of the interest attack of interest and political communication.
  • So while talking about the categories he focused on three major functions of the policymaking that is rule creation, rule application and rule decision and therefore man believes that a stable and efficient political system transforms inputs into outputs.

Systematic Comparison of Political Systems

  • So in brief we can say that a comparative study of politics and the government extend the political system from the constitution to executives, parliaments, parties to election laws and the process with the relationship that explain the immutability and change of political economy, culture, conflict, government, rights and public policies.
  • Comparative politics includes systematic study and the comparison of the world’s political system and learn about the differences and similarities between countries.
  • In contrast to journalism reports on a single country, comparative politics is primarily concerned with the discovering patterns, processes and regularities between political systems. look for trends, changes in the patterns and try to make general hypothesis that define these trends.
  • So it aims to make such comparison thoroughly and systematically without exing personal prodigized and philosophical access.
  • It include diligence, clear thinking, careful and thorough scholarship and clear consistent and balanced writing.
