Military Rule in Pakistan – Phase 1 (1958–1971)

Video Part 2 · starts at 6:57:30

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • That is at the end of this session, you will be able to define the different categories in phase one.
  • You will also be able to describe several events linked to phase one and their consequences.

Martial Law Under Iskander Mirza

  • What is a phase one?
  • Pakistan’s first commitment to civilian rule ended with martial law under President Iskander Mirza executed by General Muhammad Ayub Khan.
  • Tired of political impulses and economic stagnation, the public was ready to welcome changes that promised stability in progress.
  • Ayub Khan who quickly replaced Iskander Mirza in a coupe promised to restore democracy but instead took greater authority for himself and was unable to bring about democracy or progress.
  • This set the pattern for all military rulers after seizing power from elected leaders in Pakistan.
  • Pakistan’s problems would have been a challenge for any administration.
  • Within a decade of his scoop, the demand for freedom brought about a temporary restoration of civilian rule.
  • Still, Pakistan remained volatile and its violent quest for identity and fate led to the 1971 departure of the East Wing. a country that declared independence as Bangladesh.
  • The use of military forced by the west wing to end the rebellion led to a war with India which with the help of the former east wing firmly defeated Pakistani troops.
  • Both the loss of territory in the war with India shook the Pakistani soul.

Ayub Khan Seizes Power

  • Ayub Khan seizes power within three weeks of President Iskander Mirza’s martial law to maintain power over the legislature on October 7th, 1958.
  • Supreme Commander Muhammad Ayub Khan seized power in a military coup.
  • Ayub Khan was a Pashtun which comprises region of Punjab in the NWFP closer to its more urban cultural influence.
  • He joined the British Indian army like his father and with by the time Britain withdrew 1947 Au Khan rose to the rank of brigadier general one of the highest Muslims in the cops.
  • During the division, he was appointed a member of the boundary commission and was responsible for maintaining order in conflict areas where borders had not yet been determined.
  • He was later accused of being lazy in preventing the violence that these areas during the division.

Ayub Khan: Distrust of Politicians

  • After the split, Ayub Khan was in charge of the East Pakistan Army and insulted politicians and politicians as he later said in his autobiography, Friends not masters.
  • He believed that politicians were corrupt and the political system was inherently inefficient.
  • He was a professional military man far from the difficult world of Pakistan’s politics and supported a structure in which orders were undoubtedly obeyed.
  • He became convinced the democracy had to be restricted in order to avoid adverse effects in a fledgling nation.
  • This view will be the basis of his basic democracy governance policy since 1959.

Ayub Khan’s Rise & Public Support

  • In 1950, Liaquat Ali Khan asked Ayub Khan to become the country’s supreme commander and in 1954 he became minister of defense.
  • Many Pakistanis who were dissatisfied with the civilian government supported a coupe against Iskander Mirza and Ayub Khan used this goodwill to bring about major reforms in the political, social and economic structure.
  • The corrupt administrator was tried as part of his first demonstration of government reform.
  • A total of about 3,000 civil servants were dismissed and more civil servants were demoted.
  • The military administration also promugated an election institution disqualification order which allowed the government to ban selected politicians from serving in electoral.

The Basic Democracies Order (1959)

  • On October 27th, 1959, Ayub Khan introduced a democracy order.
  • It planned to replace a currently suspended 1956 constitution’s democratic parliamentary system with an indirect selective government system.
  • This doctrine was based in Ayub Khan’s belief that the largely illiterate population of Pakistan requires a more limited form democracy with reduced political and social freedom.
  • The system was intended to slowly introduce the government’s work to the population by limiting its decision-making power to local governments and rural development.
  • Aayou Khan called the form of governance as basic democracy and others called it a representational dictator.
  • The order created 80,000 election council seats the owners of which were responsible for the election of high-ranking government officials.
  • The public voted for 80,000 representatives, members of the village and town council and the elected members of parliament.
  • Ayub Khan has also embarked on a major land reform in West Pakistan to curb the impact of landlords whose concentration of wealth and power was considered a threat to the government and an obstacle to economic development.
  • These reforms limited the size of land ownership to thousand acres on non-errigated land and 500 acres on irrigated land Islamabad.

Moving the Capital from Karachi to Islamabad

  • After the 1959 coupe, General Ayub Khan decided to relocate the capital from Karachi to the inland.
  • Karachi is an international city whose celebrities of business industry leaders. Many Mujah refugees from India lived here.
  • They were often more educated, more literate and a more secular view of events and news of the inland people of Pakistan.
  • Aayub Khan appointed a committee under Agha Muhammad Yahya Khan, chief of staff of the army to recommend the location of the new capital and the commission proposed a location just north of Rawalpindi.

The Indus Waters Settlement

  • The Indus water dispute resolution in December 1958.
  • Pakistan finally settled a long-standing dispute between Pakistan and India over the waters of the Indus River and unconditionally accepted the world’s bank plan to divide the water.
  • With Pakistan’s approval, the Indus waters treaty was drafted and signed in September 1960.
  • It gave Pakistan exclusive rights to the Indus and Chenab rivers in West Punjab.
  • India was granted exclusive rights to the Ravi bees and Sap rivers.
  • In addition, during the 10-year transition period, India was to supply Pakistan with water from eastern rivers, Pakistan took advantage of the transition period to build and refurge a large water project.

Military Rule in Pakistan – Phase 2 (1977–1999)

Video Part 2 · starts at 7:06:25

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • At the end of this session you will be able to describe the martial law of Zia and politics under him.
  • You will also be able to describe Zia-ul-Haq in Afghanistan war.
  • You’ll be able to describe Islamization duties of the federal Sharia court.

Zia-ul-Haq & Martial Law (1977)

  • The downfall of Zia-ul-Haq.
  • Now the martial law of Zia-ul-Haq in 1977 by Zia declared martial law suspended the 1973 constitution and became the chief martial law administrator.
  • As with previous martial law, the federal and state cabinets as well as national and state parliaments were dissolved.
  • The prime minister and opposition leader were arrested and the president of Pakistan was allowed to continue his position as head of state and the Supreme Court secretary of the state high court was given the position of governor of the state.
  • The military council was placed with a right to act as supreme decision body.
  • As under AUB, Pakistan was once again divided into five martial law zones governed by the chief martial law administrator.

Postponed Elections & Bhutto

  • Zia Ulhak declared that Pakistan’s democratic rule would be strengthened within 90 days.
  • However, the election was postponed as a release of Zulfikar Ali Bhutto brought the masses to his demonstrations and he announced that it would not be an easy victory for military aid parties such as the Pakistan National Alliance.
  • As a result, the military decided to hold a nonpartisan election in which PPP members succeeded in winning most of the seats.
  • In 1985, General Zia-ul-Haq held a general election as promised in martial law.
  • However, despite the elections in which Prime Minister Muhammad Khan Junejo came to power, Zia remained influential in civilian government.
  • In 1988, Zia-ul-Haq dismissed a carefully selected prime minister due to the orury blast between Zia and Junejo and tensions of the Afghanistan issue with Junejo was off the line.
  • Politics under Zia-ul-Haq Zia mainly emphasized Islamization and did not abolish the 1973 constitution.

Restrictions on Political Activity & the Proposed Constitutional Guidance

  • At the beginning of his administration, Haq imposed restrictions on the political activities that caused political demonstrations against Ministry Ja.
  • Zia-ul-Haq also submitted a proposal for a constitutional guarantee that would allow the military to share decision-making power with the political elite at the national level.
  • He further decided to establish the National Security Council which consisted of 11 members in power to play a direct role in constitutional political settings.
  • Military officers were also given Pakistani district administration group and diplomatic department position causing anger among civilians taking competitive exams for these positions.

The Afghan War

  • The Afghanistan war.
  • Zia Olak was known to have played an important role during the war between Afghanistan and Soviet Union.
  • Zia engaged in the Afghan war because of the idealistic spectrum of supporting the majid as jihad and eventually spreading the Islamic revolution the republic of central Asia.
  • Relations between Afghanistan and Pakistan were at stake to the Pashtun issue and needed to build an alliance with the west with the help of weapons to reduce India’s supremacy over Pakistan.
  • Soviet invasion helped India secure expanding Indian Soviet trade relations and India’s reliance on Soviet weapons.
  • This led Pakistan to go for an alliance with the United States.

US Aid, Islamisation & the Nuclear Programme

  • Zia-ul-Haq received 3.2 billion from Washington in 6 years and was able to escape the pressure of the United States to return to democracy and defend human rights.
  • Zia-ul-Haq was also involved in the launch of the nuclear program and made a lot of money from the war in Afghanistan. Islamization.
  • Zia-ul-Haq claims that former political civilian leader failed to create an Islamic state enforce Sharia law.
  • As a result, Islamization was one of his prime goals and the administration was aiming to achieve it.
  • Zia applied the Hanafi interpretation to Islamic law creating tensions in Shia and Sunnis in Pakistan.
  • Tensions between Shias and Sunnis intensified not only because of differences with internal dominations but also because of the KIN revolution in Iran.
  • The Islamization program covered areas such as judicial reform, Islamic criminal law and educ economic and educational activities.
  • Beginning with the 1981 judiciary, Haq weakened the judiciary by imposing a provisional constitutional order that required all judges to make a new pledge of allegiance.

Courts & Delays in Justice

  • Though there were many courts that combined Kazzy in the Commonwealth, there was little justice.
  • The cases were brought to court, but it took forever to resolve them. Duties of the Federal Shariat Court.
  • One of the duties of the federal Sharia court was to make a decision on an appeal by a lower court in accordance with 1979 Hudood Ordinance which imposed penalties in accordance with the Quran and Sunna.

Islamic Criminal Code & Its Punishments

  • The crimes are covered by Islamic criminal code on murder, adultery, perjury and addiction.
  • Although the punishment for these crimes was severe, strict Islamic evidence rules were required and therefore possibility of conviction decreased significantly.
  • Nevertheless, the punishment for theft led to many uprisings of human rights activists and doctors as it involved cutting the hands of criminals who violated individual rights.
  • In addition, the adultery verdict also caused a lot of controversy because the punishment consists of either stoning or 100 whipping in public, but the rape punishment included only imprisonment. The downfall of Haq.

Opposition to Islamisation: The 1981 Movement

  • In the 1980s, radical Shia feminist and opposition protested against the Islamization policy.
  • In 1981, political parties gathered to launch the movement for restoration of democracy.
  • There were few groups such as the Balti separatists.
  • Yet the MRD symbolized by the Pashtun people’s party for controlling the turmoil caused by this movement.
  • Three divisions of soldiers were needed and three weeks of crackdown alone killed 189 and recorded nearly 2,000 prisoners.
  • However, MRD boycotted the elections and blamed the legislative elections were enhanced for Zia.
  • During his parliamentary session, Zia appointed himself as president, appointed Sindhi Muhammad Khan Junejo as prime minister.
  • In December 1985, martial law was lifted and political parties resurfaced.
  • The eth amendment of the constitution gave Zia the right to dismiss the government and he dismissed Prime Minister Junejo in 1988.
  • In 1988, Z and the US ambassador died in a plane crash like Pakistan to return to a multi-party parliamentary democracy.

Military Rule in Pakistan – Phase 3 (1999–2008)

Phases 1 & 2: Recap

  • In the few earlier subtopics we read and understood about the phase one and phase two of military rule in Pakistan.
  • But in this subtopic particularly we’re going to read and understand about the phase three.
  • Describe the referendum in LFO 2002, the general elections of 2002 in its aftermath.
  • The 17th constitutional amendment in local government election 2005. Phase three.
  • Sudden attempts by Prime Minister Nawaz Sharif to dismiss army chief in his absence gave the military the opportunity to take over the civilian government on October 1999 without violent clashes.

Musharraf’s Seven-Point Agenda

  • After taking over the duties of Pakistan CEOs, General Musharraf imposed urgent rules under the PCO and suspended the constitution with parliament in 1999.
  • In his speech, he announced his sevenpoint agenda.
  • Rebuilding public confidence, strengthening federation, eliminate eliminating in congruity, resurrection of economy, ensuring law and order, swift justice, depoliticization of state institutions, delegation of power and wide accountability.
  • Immediately after the military coupe, a negative international reaction was brought to the military regime.
  • The Commonwealth of Nations suspended its membership in Pakistan.
  • General Musharrafif then appointed obedient judges who supported the administration and protected future legal issues.
  • On May 2000, the Supreme Court confirmed through various petitions that the army had taken over under the National Emergency Evacuation Doctrine.
  • After the verdict, the military gained power and confidence at the expense of judicial independence.

Devolution of Power Plan (2001)

  • In June 2001, Musharraf issued the presidential succession to replace President Muhammad Rafiq Tarar.
  • The devolution of powers plan to decentralize system and delegate authority.
  • General Musharraf established the national reconstruction authority under the chairmanship of General Tan Nakri.
  • The NRB had created a delegation plan in which local governments were to be elected on a nonpartisan basis and were to eventually function in August 2001.
  • The pattern of local government was present in all state except Kentton region which was under military control. Devolution of powers.
  • Then the new system empowered elected Nazim at the expense of bureaucracy and state and national level politicians. Local administration was further politicized.
  • As a result, already weak institutions became more vulnerable.
  • The subordination of bureaucracy and abolition of former administration system cause a lot of harm and gaps in cooperative relationship between bureaucrats and elected representatives.

Accountability Mechanisms

  • The accountability mechanism.
  • Another step is taken towards accountability by reorganizing the already functioning ethas commission into the national accountability bureau under the chair of General Ajmat.
  • The NAB was tasked with investigating the corruption of politicians.
  • But after a while, General Amjud was asked to elevate concerns about the finance department, civil servants and other organizations.
  • The files of known corrupt politicians were closed and the loyalty was transferred to the progovernment party.
  • Opposition politicians were involved in many accidents.
  • This led to accusations that NAB became a partisan organization.
  • The NAB ordinance was also challenged by Supreme Court that it did not empower the constitution.
  • However, the government was instructed to make some amendments to any of the ordinance with instructions contained in the ruling. The event of 911 and its implications.

The September 11 Attacks & Pakistan

  • The September 11, 2001 terrorist attacks in the United States changed the political scenario of the world and had a major impact on the policies of Pakistan.
  • By changing that strategy, the United States showed off Pakistan stability and strengthened Musharraf’s position.
  • The United States also provided enormous economic resources that ignored Pakistan’s nuclear program.
  • As Musharraf was already responsible for the decision, he lost his determination to oppose Americans and accepted all the demands including reversing Afghanistan’s policies. Referendum 2002.
  • Meanwhile, a referendum was held in 2002 justifying his position in gaining the legitimacy of a long stay.
  • The irregularities committed during the referendum diminished the favorable image built by his reforms.
  • Some public misconduct was emphasized, including some people casting a lot of votes, employees obliged to vote, people being able to vote at any polling place.
  • After the referendum, he promulgated a 2002 legal framework order.

Constitutional Amendments & the 2002 Elections

  • By applying the constitutional amendment powers given by the Supreme Court, the president was empowered to dissolve parliament, appoint governors, appoint military commanders and national security council members were also appointed. The general elections of 2002.
  • After the promulgation of LFO, Musharraf declared that a general election would be held at the end of 2002.
  • The party was finally announced and then on June 28, the political party amendment tax was issued enforcing new eligibility requirements for candidates and political parties. The general election was held in 2002.
  • All political parties decided to participate in the election and completed and competed for 272 seats at the National Assembly of Pakistan.
  • The PMLQ was also the largest political party in the Senate and its chief Muhammad manu was elected speaker of the house.

Economics & Free-Market Reforms in Chile

Military Takeover & Economic Change

  • A few subtopics earlier we read and understood about the various other subconits of the country Chile.
  • After the military hijacked the government in September 1973, a period of dramatic economic changes began.
  • With strong government intervention, Chile gradually transformed from economy isolated from the rest of the world to a liberalized global integrated economy.
  • There the power of the market was free to guide most of the economic decisions. During this period, inflation fell sharply.
  • The government deficits was virtually eliminated.
  • The economy went through dramatic liberalization of the foreign sector and a strong market system was established.
  • From an economic point of view, the era of General August pino can be divided into two eras.
  • The first is from 1973 to 1982 which corresponds to the time when most of France was implemented.
  • This period ended with the international death crisis and the collapse of the Chilean economy.

Phase Two (1982–1990): Economic Recovery

  • The second phase 1982 to 1990s is characterized by economic recovery and strengthening of free market reform.
  • Chile experienced the free market revolution initiated by the Pinocheta administration in 1970s and 1980s.
  • After the social democratic government regained democracy, a very similar model was adopted with some modifications.
  • This economic revolution liberated entrepreneurship and the ability to generate wealth and today the country at one of the highest per capita income levels in Latin America.
  • The postio democratic government focused on leading peaceful return to democracy by continuing growth in the economy and reducing income poverty.
  • The general consensus is that it worked very well in these respects.

The Chicago Boys & the Failed Marxist Experiment

  • Following the failed Marxist experiment by Chilean President Salvador Allen Day, the so-called Chicago boys led free market revolution of 1970s and 1980s.
  • In fact, from 1990 to 2010, a leftwing coalition called the Constitution came to power.
  • The consultation maintained a foundation of free market system despite being made above opponents of the military dictatorship.
  • For western counterweight, Chile’s economic reform was a symbolic victory in the battle between socialism and progressivism.
  • As historian Niall Ferguson points out, the backash against welfare began in Chile.
  • moreover, according to Ferguson, Chile’s economic reforms such as the privatization of social security system were much more radical than those attempted in the United States, the center of the free market economy.
  • In line with the same policy, William Ratliff and Robert Packenham stated that Chile was a pass from socialism and extreme state capitalism.
  • For Marxist intellectual David Harvey, the first experiment in the formation of the neoliberal state took place in Chile after the Pinochet chronic inflation which peaked above 500 in 1973 was below 10% by the 1990s. between 1975 and 2015. Chile per capita income quadruple.

Improvements in Living Standards

  • Life expectancy increased in 69 to 79 years and house overcrowding decreased.
  • The middle class defined by the World Bank increased.
  • On average, access to high education increased fivefold over the same period, mainly benefiting the bottom fifth and access to higher education increased eightfold.
  • Between 1990 and 2015, the wealthiest 10% income increased.
  • Other measures of inequality also show the narrowing of the gap between the wealthy and the rest of the population.
  • In short, thanks to the free market reforms introduced by the Chicago boy and maintained by the later democratic regime, Chile has become the most prosperous country in Latin America with primary benefits to the population.

  • One candidate will be elected from the winning coalition and the second candidate will be elected from the runnerup coalition.

Safeguards in the Constitution Against the Left

Systems Approach – Definitions, Historical Context & Characteristics

Video Part 2 · starts at 8:01:15

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • That is at the end of this session, you would be able to define the concept of system approach.

Definitions & Historical Context of the Systems Approach

  • The definitions the systems approach the Indian investment philosophers historical context of systems approach and the characteristic feature of system analysis.
  • Describe Indian investment philosophers viewpoints and system approach.
  • Now you would be wondering what is the concept of systems approach?
  • Traditionally the study of comparative politics has been called as comparison.
  • This includes studying the political systems that exist in different states.
  • Here we compare the characteristics, strengths, weaknesses, similarities and dissimilarities of political system.
  • The system approach is a study of interrelated variables that form a system. a unit, a whole consisting of many facts and a set of interacting elements.
  • This approach assumes that the system is composed of identifiable, regular, and internally consistent patterns each interacting with each other and giving an overall picture of the self-regulating order.
  • Therefore, this is a study of a series of interactions that occur within a larger system that are amalytically different.
  • David Easton, Gabriel Almond, G. B. Powell, Morton Kaplan, Karl Deutsch are behavioral scholars conducted system theory to analyze political phenomena and develop political theory in the late 1950s and 80s.
  • In the early 20th century, systems theory was first applied to biology by Ludwig von Bertalanffy.
  • It was more important for them to know what role the behavioral patterns of a particular social system played in maintaining the system as a whole and not how it originated.

Logical Positivists & the Vienna Circle

  • Logical positivist such as Moritz Schlick, Rudolf Carnap, Otto Neurath, Victor Kraft and Herbert Feigl considered empirically observable and verifiable knowledge to be the only valid knowledge.
  • But Hervin Ferman and others inspired the writings of modern political philosophers.
  • David Easton, Gabriel Almond, G. Bingham Powell called these such were are and still consider to be he to be popular behavioral scholars.
  • Now in the concept of system analysis there are various inputs there are various elements.

The Political System as a System of Interaction

  • Firstly system it is useful to see political life and system of action.
  • Political system is described as a set or system of interaction defined by the fact that they are less directly related to the authoritative distribution of value of a society.
  • The system is distinguishable from the environment in which it resides and is susceptible to its influence.
  • Those aspects of society outside the boundaries of the political system can be generalized by stating that they’re composed of all other subsystems of society.
  • They make up the environment of the political system. The third element is the boundary.
  • Analytically, the boundaries of all system may be interpreted as criteria that they included in excluded from systems that form the focus of interest.
  • In the case of the political system, this test is whether the interaction is more or less directly related to the authoritative allocation of value to society.
  • Input is an indicator that summarizes most of the important crossber effects between the political system and other systems belonging to its environment.
  • According to Gabriel Almond there because there are various definitions the systems approach.
  • The political system of society is a legal order maintaining a transformation system.
  • Wise men argue that all political system includes the political structure, actors or roles performed by their agents, the patterns of interaction that exist between individuals or groups and the political processes.

The Systems Approach as a Way of Looking at Politics

  • Katlan’s political system also has recognizable multivariate benefits.
  • As for JC Jooi, an approach may be defined as a way of looking at and explaining a particular phenomena.
  • While the normativism that characterized a traditional approach represents a valuable system, the empirical theory that characterizes the modern approach does the same with factfilled systems.
  • As per professor Esen Ray, the application of the system thought allows us to see the subject in ways in which each part of political canvas is not independent but related to each other.

Micro-Analysis & Interdisciplinary Fusion

  • We can also say that operation of one part cannot be fully understood without reference to the way in which the whole itself operates.
  • It the system theory gives us an excellent opportunity for fusing microanalytical studies with macroanalytical ones.
  • The concept developed by this theory opens to new questions and creates a new dimension for investigating the political process that often facilitates communication of insights and perspectives in other areas.
  • The historical concept general systems theory has positioned itself as interdisciplinary and has interdisciplinary interactions.
  • At the heart of this language were the concept of a system defined as a group of interacting interdependent elements that formed a complex whole.
  • He also presented a new world view with that emphasizes key concepts such as the incorporation of all systems into other larger systems and the dynamic and ever-changing processes of self-organization, growth and adaptation.
  • General systems theory was generally interpreted as equilibrium oriented and essentially static as interpreted in functional sociology especially in the mid- 20th century.

Systems Theory in Comparative Politics

  • The introduction of systems like other approaches in comparative politics by writers such as eastern Gabriel Almond and Kaplan is in fact a reaction to the traditional tendency of one-dimensionalism in the process of patterning scientific analysis.
  • The systems approach is one of the latest approaches to help us understand political activity and behavior more clearly than before.
  • It sees social phenomena as a series of such inter relationships.
  • System analysis covers virtually all social sciences, not just political science.
  • System analysis means the system as a series of interactions.
  • The existence of a system means that it exists through its elements that is object.
  • The systems that make up the elements of a larger system are called subsystem.
  • The settings in which the system occurs are called environment.
  • The line that separates the system from its environment is called a boundary and the system takes input from the environment and support its functionality.
  • Therefore, the system approach has its own characteristics.

Interrelated Social Phenomena

  • Social phenomena do not exist in isolation.
  • It’s not just about joining many parts together to make up the whole thing.
  • That part may not be organically interrelated and is not really related but it constitutes a whole in the sense that it interacts and is interrelated.
  • Specific behavioral relationships pattern them into a living system.
  • It operates through input and output mechanism influences it and then operates within an environment that provides feedback to the environment.
  • Its main concern is how it can do its best to maintain itself and face the challenges of decline and decline.

The Developmental Approach & Variables

  • What is the political system derivated by Almond?
  • The developmental approach claims to know about a set of variables that are important among other characteristics of the political system as well as the limits and possibilities.
  • Developmental approaches not only explain, compare and explain but also predict the political changes in development that occur in the political system.
  • According to Almond and Powell, the theory specifies relationships between variables so that they can make predictions about the outcome of interactions between variables. it can be tested and further reformulated.
  • They believe that their approach also allows us to lay the foundation explanations as well as the prediction.
  • We are aware of the limitations and limitations of the political system and associate political development with political structure and culture.
  • In the developmental approach, political development is measured by role differentiation and subsystem independence and political culture is measured by secularization.
  • Political culture also includes the socialization process.
  • The approach toward scientific theory draw certain important theoretical conclusions by analyzing political systems at three levels and interconnecting them.
  • And has these three levels as conversion, capability and classification of political systems.
  • The political system also faces four types of developmental problems.

Variables: State-Building, Nation-Building & Participation

  • State building, nation building, participation, distribution.
  • They all affect the depend development variables of differentiation, secularization, and subsystem independence.
  • They form a turning point in the path of political development.
  • In other words, the political system faces the problems of unity and control, group identity and loyalty, the involvement of members of society in the decision-making process and the distribution of goods and services to people through these gradual increases.

Utility of the Almond–Powell Model

  • What is the utility of Almond and Powell model?
  • Gabriel Almond’s brilliant innovation was to outline an approach to understanding the political system considering it structural components, its institution as well as the functioning throughout the system.
  • Prior to structural functionalism, scholars had no way of systematically comparing different political systems beyond the basic and often inconclusive analysis of their system.
  • At the most basic level, the model of structural functionalism assumes that the political system is composed of systems such as interested party groups, political parties, executive branches, legislative and judicial departments, and bureaucracy.
  • For almond, a more complete understanding only appears when they begin to look at how the system behaves in the political process.
  • As he explained, the stakeholder groups help clarify political issues.
  • The party then aggregate and represent them in a consistent and meaningful way.
  • The government then enacts public policies to address them.

The System in Dynamic Relationship with Others

  • This model does explain what happens within the political system, but the system is never completely self-contained.
  • They exist in a dynamic relationship with other political systems and must continuously adapt to changing situations in a larger sociopolitical context.
  • For this reason, all political systems requires an efficient feedback mechanism.
  • Political socialization as fasment and power refers to the process by which culture passes on the values, beliefs and habits of citizens of the next generation.
  • It refers to the almost unconscious process by which families, schools, communities, political parties and other socializing actors instill the dominant political values of culture.
  • Recruitment refers to the way citizens actively participate in the political system and communication represents how the political system disseminates information that is essential for its proper functioning.

Limitations of the Almond–Powell Model

  • There are certain limitations of the power model.
  • The structural and functional approach has some inherent bias normative implications. It is conservative in nature.
  • It recognizes the primary purpose of the political system is to ensure its own survival.
  • For this reason, there is no particular reaction beyond innovations and movements aimed in political change, that is those that enhance adaptability and resilience.
  • There is a democratic and participatory bias as long as citizens opinions and involvement in political processes are seen as the shortest route to political stability and response.
  • Critics point out that the system is primarily concerned with its own survival and that the analysis is based in democratic political style in the west.

Weaknesses of Almond’s Structural Functionalism

  • Critics also point out some weaknesses in Gabriel Almond’s structural functional approach.
  • Gabriel Almond defines a political system as a series of interactions found in all independent societies.
  • But he doesn’t explain anywhere about which societies are independent or can be treated as independent societies.
  • It ignores the study of course political systems that are functioning throughout each society.
  • Almond arbitrarily classifies political communication as an input function.
  • Political communication works at all three levels input, conversion and output.

The Neglect of the Feedback Function

  • Gabriel Almond’s model of political system ignores the importance of feedback function.
  • He envisions feedback as part of the communication process.
  • Gabriel Almond’s first model is a static model with the commitment to maintain the status quo. The concept of equilibrium is in inadequate.
  • Gabriel Almond’s approach can be applied to the developed political system of the west, not to the developing political system of Asia or Africa.
  • Gabriel Almond’s concept of political development use variables and concepts drawn from the theory of western liberal democratic political system to conceptualize political development.

Samuel Huntington’s Concept of Political Decay

Video Part 2 · starts at 8:39:40

🎯 Learning Objectives

Mobilisation vs Institutionalisation

  • What was the Samuel Huntington’s concept of political decay in political development and political decline?
  • Samuel Huntington explores the conflict between political mobilization and institutionalization and the importance of institutional development in democratization.
  • What is common in many developing countries is the fact that political participation is growing much faster than formal political systems.
  • In many developing societies, the conflict between mobilization and institutionalization is of utmost political concern.
  • Despite the increasing importance of political institutionalism, much of the literature written about developing countries tends to ignore that idea for the most part.
  • Instead, political scientists tend to emphasize the process of modernization and the idea of increased social mobilization, political participation.

Political Development as Modernisation

  • Samuel Huntington first explores the concept of political development as modernization.
  • The definition of political development varies but most share two close related characteristics.
  • The first feature is that growth is synonymous with the idea of modernization.
  • Therefore, political development is also defined as political modernization.
  • Second, modernization and development are broad topics covering different disciplines.
  • So there are many ways to measure political development.
  • In addition, the definition of political development tends to itemize many different criteria.
  • There are four categories in all definitions of political development.

Rationalisation & Nationalism as Criteria

  • The first feature is a rationalization feature which focuses on functional differentiation and success criteria.
  • The second criterion is nationalism which emphasize nation state and nation building as a fundamental aspect of political development.
  • The third criterion is the idea of democratization which essentially focuses on competition and equality of power.
  • The final criterion is mobilization focused on political participation.

Participation & Political Decay

  • Political participation stipulates the greater the level of development the greater the level of modernization.
  • Political decay was the first political theory written by Samuel P. Huntington explaining how turmoil and chaos arise from social modernization which increases faster through political and institutional modernization.
  • Huntington provides different definitions of political development and describes forms of political decline according to these definitions.
  • Huntington focuses primarily on political development as modernization and institutionalization.
  • However, he points out the different definitions of political development, arbitrary ways to understand the rise of political systems and the relationships in political systems in different country.
  • Huntington identifies two characteristics of political development.
  • First, development is synonymous with modernization.
  • So, political development can be defined as political modernization.

Criteria for Measuring Political Development

  • Second, modernization and development is a very broad topic and covers many areas.
  • So, there are many criteria for measuring political development.
  • The four generally agreed criteria for determining the first are rationalization which includes a transition from specifism to universalism or a focus on functional differentiation and success criteria from political point of view.
  • The third criterion is to focus on democratization which is essentially focused on competition and equality of power.
  • According to Huntington’s definition of modernization of political development, political decline is the opposite of the linear idea of social progress.
  • But within the model of modernization, social retreat is impossible.
  • Instead, modern and modernizing states can change by losing their abilities and by acquiring them, resulting in political decline.
  • In addition, the acquisition of certain capabilities usually occurs that comes with the cost of other capabilities.
  • There are certain disadvantages of Huntington’s model.

Huntington’s Starting Point

  • Huntington begins with Tokyo’s expression.
  • In order for a man to maintain a civilized civilization, he must grow art that connects at the same rate as increasing equality of conditions.
  • Firstly, we need to investigate Tokubal’s expression that Huntington refers to as a headline.
  • That is because the art of association and the equality of conditions cannot progress to the same extent.

Competing Priorities in Political Development

  • Given that there are priorities here, it seems that it is not possible to prioritize for reasons.
  • In other words, if you prioritize political involvement, the art of connecting is a bit slow and it’s very natural to come from behind. Disadvantages of Huntington’s model.
  • Institutionalization is impossible without mobilization.
  • In fact, mobilization results from many sociological physiological, economic, cultural, religious reasons and is usually an unforeseen sequence of events.
  • After the mobilization stage, the necessary political systems, institutions emerge naturally.
  • moreover, since society is like a living thing, it is against the logic of social science to first institutionalize it and expect mobilization from the opposite perspective.
  • Society plays a role according to the general world order and undergo the changes necessary for this role.
  • Just as organisms are in different states depending on environmental conditions in a natural process.

Mobilisation Leads to Political Decline

  • Given increased mobilization, political involvement is a main science of modernization.
  • Huntington believes the result is political decline, not political development.
  • It is also arguable to use the term economic growth in Huntington’s argument that rapid economic growth leads to political incompetence.
  • If you look closely, Huntington uses the concept of economic growth.
  • Economic growth means that the country increases its poor resources, pushes the limits of production facilities by improving the quality of these resources or reaches higher production levels by changing production technology institutional frameworks.
  • In other few subtopics, further we’ll be learning and understanding more about other philosophers in system analysis and functional and structural approaches.

Critique of Developmentalists – David Apter & Samuel Huntington

Who Are the Developmentalists?

  • Who are the developmentalists and what have they criticized?
  • Modernization theory believes that attempts at rapid economic, social and cultural change in a nation must be accompanied by political development.
  • The impact in traditional groups which have changed slowly and intentionally over the centuries and suddenly face rapid modernization is enormous.
  • For traditional societies, experiencing this collection of economic, social and cultural changes without serious difficulties is not an easy task.
  • As for Samuel Huntington, rapid social change and the rapid mobilization of new groups into politics coupled with the slow development of the political system are the cause of violence and instability.
  • Political instability is coupled with the inability to address the issue of equality and political participation and the art of a rapidly growing society to unite society.
  • Two important aspects of modernization are economic development and social mobilization.
  • Social mobilization Samuel Huntington states the social mobilization is among other things the result of literacy, education, urbanization.
  • Social mobilization involves changing aspirations of individuals, groups and society.

Economic Development: Capacity Change

  • Economic development, it includes complete economic activity and social outcome. Economic development involves capacity changes.
  • Of all the criteria that may be used to evaluate empirical theory, three are to be particularly relevant in this case.
  • Then there are attempts to evaluate Huntington’s approach using matrix perceiving, accuracy, predictive power.

Rapid Social Mobilisation & Frustration

  • If society has not experienced periods of rapid social mobilization, individual aspirations remain stable and therefore easily satisfied. Satisfaction promotes political stability.
  • Rapid social mobilization on the other hand leads to rising expectations and thus establishes itself as an initial condition in a series of situations that ultimately threaten the political order.
  • If society’s prosperous, high aspirations cause for rapid mobilization can be met with minimal difficulty and satisfaction would promote political stability.
  • But without these conditions of influence, rising expectations would not be met.

Frustration & Political Order

  • These frustrations are unlikely to go against political order.
  • A society provides a rational outlook for improving one’s condition.
  • Energy is devoted to the progress of individuals or groups regardless of the legitimacy and process of the political system.
  • However, when mobility opportunities are lacking, these frustrations are likely to be absent, creating conditions for large scale political action.
  • With an effective political system infrastructure in place, governments can engage in such mass participation and meet the demands of change.
  • Visualizing Huntington’s explanation of political instability as a series of critical junctures certainly conveys the complexity of that explanation rather than a summary of Huntington’s own three equations.

Institutionalisation & Adaptability

  • It also shows the extent to which his apparently simple three equation model is based on a long set of unexamined assumptions and unclear hypothesis.
  • Those who look at politics from social engineering perspective see the true flow of casualty as Huntington ignores structural aspects of social mobilization such as increased education enrollment and continued broader access to the mass media.
  • The deliberate administration attempts modernization in response to the public demand to do so.
  • Hutton specified four criteria for measuring institutionalization each of which was considered a continum.
  • These were adaptability, rigidity, complexity, simplicity, autonomy, subordination, coherence, and consistency.

Institutional Adaptability & the Measurement Problem

  • He further argued that the closer the organization was to the first term of each of these pairs, the more institutionalized it was.
  • What one of the theories, the three basic problems of measurement flow directly from this conceptualization.
  • Bendor further suggests that organizations can be inflexible but still very complex.
  • moreover, it seems quite reasonable that increasing complexity can reduce adaptability.
  • We can increase these examples, but there are some basic points.
  • If the whole concept of institutionalization is equal to the sum of its parts, it is very difficult to know if that part is very interpretable.
  • This leads to the second basic difficulty associated with the concept of institutionalization.
  • Bendor is certainly correct when he points out the problem of concept becomes particularly noticeable when trying to manipulate the concept concretely.

Structural-Functional Derivative & its Criticism

Video Part 2 · starts at 8:51:44

🎯 Learning Objectives

  • That is at the end of this session you will be able to define the structural function approach.
  • You will also be able to describe its consequence and limitations.

Origins of the Structural-Functional Derivative

  • What is the structural functional derivative?
  • The structural and functional approach derives from previous use of functionalist and system models in anthropology, sociology, biology and politics.
  • Structural functionalism became widespread around 1960 when it became clear that the methods of studying US and European politics did not help study newly independent countries and that a new approach was needed.
  • Structural functionalism presupposes the existence of a bounded system and studies structure from the perspective function within the system.

What Is a Structure?

  • What is a structure?
  • Here the word structure is used in different sense than its sociological meaning.
  • Structure means institution and every political system has several institutions which is political parties, legislatores etc.
  • In structural functionalism, the structure of the political system like political parties, stakeholder groups, parliament, executives, bureaucrats are not clearly defined and properly patterned. Nevertheless, its importance is immeasurable.
  • Instin structural functional analysis determines important structures and then attempts to track the function of these structures.
  • All political systems have specific structures that cannot be confused with each other.

Radcliffe-Brown & the Structure–Function Link

  • As far as functionality is concerned, there is some overlap between the functionality of the structure, but this duplication should not be overemphasized.
  • Davies and Lewis wrote in a prominent study, structural and functional analysis can be said to have its roots in biological and mechanical sciences.
  • In social sciences, it was first used in anthropology and later primary as a mode of sociological analysis by tali.
  • Mile derky from 1858 to 1917 is treated as a hair to the long tradition of French social thought.
  • He carefully analyzed the basic structures of society, their different thoughts and different social systems and did this from a biological perspective.

Durkheim: Society as an Entity

  • According to Durkheim, society should be considered an entity.
  • There are several parts of society, all of which are well connected.
  • The parts play their assigned role, but the parts are not completely independent of each other.
  • He also saw that the systems or parts of society are very normal departments and the functions they perform are also normal.
  • Two renowned anthropologists Bronisław Malinowski and AR Radcliffe Brown were heavily influenced by Derk’s theory of organisms.
  • Radcliffe Brown believed that the concept of function applied to human society was based on the similarities between social life and organic life.
  • Radcliffe Brown’s view is summarized by Turner as follows.
  • In each society, it is possible to show structural characteristics that contribute to the maintenance of necessary solidarity.

Functionalism & the Third World

  • Structural function is to argue that all political systems, including the third world system, can be most usefully studied and compared in different political systems based on how different structures perform these functions.
  • Structural functionalism is based on a system model.
  • And for the purpose of analysis, the political system is considered a nation state and the environment consists of the interaction of events with both domestic and external economic social political variables.
  • The idea is that there are many parties in the political system of a country and the actions of all these parties influence not only the system but also each other.
  • This theory has been accused of being conservative and statuscentric in his outlook.
  • There are no objective criteria for measuring the conditions of political system sustainability and concepts such as roles and functions are not properly defined.
  • The concept of compulsory universal functioning of the political system is arbitrary.
  • The theory is biased and politically motivated because it seeks to idolize a western liberal democratic political system while analyzing the non-western political system.

Criticism of Almond’s Structural Functionalism

  • Gabriel Almond strongly advocated in finally crafted structural functionalism has some drawbacks.
  • Critics say Almond borrowed key elements and aspects of his structural functionalism primarily from sociology.
  • Cesi from Parson the most prominent sociologist of the late 20th century.
  • The problem is that the terms and concepts that are abundantly related to sociology may not be the same in political science.
  • However, Almond structural functionalism has achieved that.
  • So, the sociological terms applied in political science do not have the proper meaning and significance.
  • Critics are of the view that this method of analysis complicates the subject.
  • Gabriel Almond structural functionalism can best be called a new attempt to see politics state.

Almond’s Model & System Interaction

  • It can be called a model, nothing more.
  • There are many factors behind the interaction between systems, but it’s a shame he didn’t pay attention to these factors.
  • We believe that all the needs to be positively considered for the purpose of comprehensive analysis and general systems theory otherwise general system theory remains incomplete.
  • The most serious accusation against Almond is that he secretly upheld the existing structure of the capitalist system.
  • He wants to establish that the capitalist system can protect itself through its management and self-regulatory mechanisms.
  • It is an excellent system compared to other systems.

The Effects of Global Factors on States

  • Today, political, cultural, economic and other factors can affect different states very easily. This effect is not one way.
  • As a result, Gabriel Almond structural functionalism has entered a new dimension in this era of globalization.
  • Western capitalist nation influence and control third world states in a variety of ways.
  • We cannot deny the fact that general system theory has opened new perspectives in comparative politics.
  • Aristotle is considered by many to be the founder of comparative politics.
  • But Gabriel Almond should be credited with expanding his foundation surrounding.
  • To give Almond justice, it must be said that it was Almond that modernized and popularized the concept of comparative politics.
  • And in the next few other subtopics, you’ll be learning more about the consequence of various approaches.

Morton Kaplan’s Systems Approach

Video Part 2 · starts at 8:59:26

🎯 Learning Objectives

The Systems Approach in International Politics

  • The system approach can be seen as a useful approach to the study of international politics.
  • It can be used to get a complete picture of relation relations between nations.
  • It can be used for both macroscopic and microscopic studies of regional subsystem that form part of the international system.
  • The concept of the system can be used to achieve the purpose of theory building in international politics.
  • The emergence of a systematic approach to the study of international politics can be explained as one of the most important developments of the 20th century.
  • Immediately after its birth, the system approach attracted the attention of many modern political scientists.
  • The systems approach seeks to analyze international affairs as a system of interdependent and interrelated interactions.
  • It studies international affairs, a system of behavior of international actors.

Countries Acting & Reacting in the International Environment

  • Countries act and react in international environment and the behaviors characterize the regularity.
  • It affects the international environment and is itself influenced by the international environment.
  • National action is a two-way activity that inherits from and gives to the international environment said neckly land.
  • National behavior affects and is affected by the international environment.

Kaplan’s International System

  • Morton Kaplan considers an international system to be an analytical entity for explaining the actions of international actors and the regulatory integrated and disruptive consequences of their policies.
  • K dies said the international system consists of clusters of settlements, means of transportation, cultural centers, linguistic territories, class and cast divisions, barriers between markets and sharp regional differences in wealth and interdependence.
  • The international system is a set of interrelated and interdependent interaction between international actors.
  • International affairs as an international system.
  • International affairs can be analyzed as a series of relationships between international parties that is as an international system.
  • The international system is not an international political system.
  • When the concept of system is used in the context of international politics, it is interpreted to mean an international system rather than an international political system.

Kaplan’s International System

  • It is not an international political system because it does not assign authoritative value.
  • The basic international units are actors of sovereign state.
  • So international actors institutions cannot formally implement their values decisions.
  • Therefore it cannot be explained as an international political system.
  • International domestic actors international actors are basically domestic actors who act in an international environment.

Kaplan & System Analysis References

  • While there are many others who have contributed to systems approach, it was Martin Kaplan who made the most comprehensive and successful characterization of politics.
  • Within the framework of system analysis references, Kaplan believes that the international system is most important of all systems in his system study systems and processes in international politics.
  • He believes that the international system has a set of actor subsystems.
  • According to Kaplan, the set of international actors can be divided into international actors and supra national actors.
  • Gender system theory especially Martin Kaplan’s six international system models has been severely criticized by some scholars.
  • Robert J. Lieber summarizes the main limitations of the system approach as follows only framework.
  • The first limitation of Easton and Captain admit is that the system approach is not yet a theory but just a conceptual framework.

Limitations of Kaplan’s Approach

  • Therefore they cannot establish the intellectual policy of international affairs inadequate.
  • The second major limitation is the deficiency of its methodology.
  • There’s a lack of operationality of the concept in a way that makes empirical testing accessible.
  • The third major limitation is the gap between theory and research. systems oriented theorizing is not allowed to move much of empirical research until very recently. Martin Kaplan’s models are limited.
  • Critics refuse to accept Martin Kaplan’s views that his model certain contains a preliminary theory of international politics. All six of his models are limited.

The Balance of Power in Modern Times

  • Power system balance is not feasible in modern times.
  • Captain was wrong to predict that the balance of the power system would lead to the lose bipolar system first and then the tip bipolar system.
  • The evolutionary process of international affairs since 1945 reflected the opposite.
  • Tight bipolar first appeared in the early 1950s and was subsequently replaced by lose bipolar or multicentricity in the 1960s.
  • The international system became a uniolar system in the 1990s.

Cybernetic Derivative of Karl Deutsch

🎯 Learning Objectives

The Political Communication Approach

  • The relatively recent and rapid development of the political communication approach in the field of scientific analysis relies heavily in the basic direction of cybernetics the science of control and communication systems.
  • This is a relatively new approach to government and political research primarily focusing in the communication process and central aspect of politics.
  • The focus of this approach is not the not on the cause or effect of such a decision but on the mechanism of how the decision is made.
  • Tal dies is one of the leading experts in communication theory and his approach is clearly demonstrated in his book govern nervousness.

What Is Cybernetics?

  • What is cybernetics?
  • Cybernetics a communication approach is another derivative system analysis.
  • Carl duch is of course sometimes referred as a leading index of cybernetics model.
  • Cybernetics is defined as a science of communication and control.
  • Its focus is a systematic study of communication and control in all types of organizations.
  • The cybernetics perspective suggests that all organizations are similar in certain fundamental characteristics and that all organizations are united by communication.
  • Since the government is an organization, the information process is mainly represented by the organization.

Information & Communication in Cybernetic Models

  • As a result, the defense concept of cybernetics approaches, especially information communication channels, is evolving.
  • The characteristics of the cybernetic model of system analysis can be simply described as models.
  • Feedback constituates an important concept of the cybernetic model which is also called a server mechanism.
  • Feedback means in German a communication network that generates action response to input information.
  • All organizations including political systems are characterized by feedback mechanism.
  • It is feedback that introduces dynamism into what would otherwise be static analysis.
  • Cybernetics introduces specific subconcepts of feedback concepts.
  • There a negative feedback, load, delay, gain, lead.
  • The political system as a system of decision making and exe and execution can be thought of as a network of communication channels.

Three Main Characteristics

  • The communication approach adopted by such has three main characteristics.
  • It no longer sees power as an important variable in explaining political phenomena.
  • Power is neither the center of politics nor the assembly of the achievement of social goods.
  • The emphasis is in the empirical nature of the concept.
  • The attempt is to update each concept through measurement and mapping.
  • Quantitative data is not considered an alternative to other types of analysis but is considered complimentary in terms of mechanisms that checks, general and confirm the judgment of historians for political analyst.

The Feedback Mechanism

  • The feedback mechanism is a very important stage in the political system.
  • Information originates in other systems and environments and is transmitted over multiple channels.
  • The information is converted back into a decision who is sent again elsewhere or to the system.
  • Information is also relevant to decision making.
  • Other systems or structures of the political system will know that the authorities have responded to the information received in the system or subsystem.
  • Further all organizations in the political system are under the control of feedback.
  • These such communication theory works only in open systems.
  • This is because only such a system as institution free to function and the inflow outflow information is done without hindrance or trouble.
  • In fact, these such communications here in Easton’s general system theory fall into the same category. Both assume an open system.

Drawbacks of the Model

  • The such model has many drawbacks.
  • It is essentially an engineering approach that describes the performance of humans in living institutions as if they machines. This approach emphasizes quantity.
  • The approach is sophisticated if it is complex and complex if it does not help us to understand the phenomena.

Drawbacks: Inflow & Outflow of Information

  • One of the key drawbacks of communication theory is that it talks a lot about the inflow and outflow information.
  • He believes that inflows outflows determine the decision-m process but the real situation tells us something else.
  • Data organizes power is a component of political activity but unfortunately he reduces importance by declaring that power was neither center of politics nor the ascent.
  • Eastern and Gabriel Almond’s general system theory indeed such as communication theory of class coordination and cooperation between classes and groups and peaceful relationship between them exists in any capitalist society and need to destroy capitalism.
  • The anti-mark sentiment albeit in a very secret form has won the disapproval of many scholars both east and west.
  • Communication theory envisioned several changes or changes aimed at the growth of society but this cannot be a radical change.
  • Keeping the existing structure setting of society intact can rely on the coordination readjustment of structure and functioning as a method meaning of social change that will bring about growth.
